## The Lineage of Christ
**Published:** 2026-05-07 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/03/the-lineage-of-christ.html)**

---

Does anyone know what we discussed last week?

* ... *

Building on that, I want to continue emphasizing that God wants to use everyone to bring forth His Will.

We will be looking at the lineage of Jesus Christ tonight. We are going to talk about the many people who have all fallen short of the glory of God, and yet God still kept them and used them in His divine plan of salvation.

We won’t discuss all of them, though I’m sure one could take a further study on each one, but I hope to discuss the people with the most familiarity to you all tonight.

We can read about the genealogy of Jesus in Matthew 1:1-17 and in Luke 3:23-28. One will notice that these two genealogies differ, and I myself didn't fully understand why. Something fascinating I came across is that the verse in Matthew traces Jesus' royal lineage through Joseph, while the one in Luke traces Mary's family tree.

Joseph's lineage goes back to David through Solomon, and Mary's lineage also goes back to David, but through David's other son, Nathan.

1The book of the genealogy of Jesus Christ, the Son of David, the Son of Abraham: 2 **Abraham** begot **Isaac** , Isaac begot **Jacob** , and Jacob begot **Judah** and his brothers. 3Judah begot **Perez** and Zerah by Tamar, Perez begot **Hezron** , and Hezron begot **Ram** . 4Ram begot **Amminadab** , Amminadab begot **Nahshon** , and Nahshon begot **Salmon** . 5Salmon begot **Boaz** by Rahab , Boaz begot **Obed** by Ruth , Obed begot **Jesse** , 6and Jesse begot **David** the king. David the king begot **Solomon** by her who had been the wife of Uriah [Bathsheba]. 7Solomon begot **Rehoboam** , Rehoboam begot **Abijah** , and Abijah begot **Asa** . 8Asa begot **Jehoshaphat** , Jehoshaphat begot **Joram** , and Joram begot **Uzziah** . 9Uzziah begot **Jotham** , Jotham begot **Ahaz** , and Ahaz begot **Hezekiah** . 10Hezekiah begot **Manasseh** , Manasseh begot **Amon** , and Amon begot **Josiah** . 11Josiah begot **Jeconiah** and his brothers about the time they were carried away to Babylon. 12And after they were brought to Babylon, Jeconiah begot **Shealtiel** , and Shealtiel begot **Zerubbabel** . 13Zerubbabel begot **Abiud** , Abiud begot **Eliakim** , and Eliakim begot **Azor** . 14Azor begot **Zadok** , Zadok begot **Achim** , and Achim begot **Eliud** . 15Eliud begot **Eleazar** , Eleazar begot **Matthan** , and Matthan begot **Jacob** . 16And Jacob begot **Joseph** the husband of Mary, of whom was born **Jesus** who is called Christ. 17So all the generations from Abraham to David are fourteen generations, from David until the captivity in Babylon are fourteen generations, and from the captivity in Babylon until the Christ are fourteen generations. Matthew 1:1-17 ~ Joseph's Lineage

23Now **Jesus** Himself began His ministry at about thirty years of age, being (as was supposed) the son of **Joseph** , the son of **Heli** , 24the son of **Matthat** , the son of **Levi** , the son of **Melchi** , the son of **Janna** , the son of **Joseph** , 25the son of **Mattathiah** , the son of **Amos** , the son of **Nahum** , the son of **Esli** , the son of **Naggai** , 26the son of **Maath** , the son of **Mattathiah** , the son of **Semei** , the son of **Joseph** , the son of **Judah** , 27the son of **Joannas** , the son of **Rhesa** , the son of **Zerubbabel** , the son of **Shealtiel** , the son of **Neri** , 28the son of **Melchi** , the son of **Addi** , the son of **Cosam** , the son of **Elmodam** , the son of **Er** , 29the son of **Jose** , the son of **Eliezer** , the son of **Jorim** , the son of **Matthat** , the son of **Levi** , 30the son of **Simeon** , the son of **Judah** , the son of **Joseph** , the son of **Jonan** , the son of **Eliakim** , 31the son of **Melea** , the son of **Menan** , the son of **Mattathah** , the son of **Nathan** , the son of **David** , 32the son of **Jesse** , the son of **Obed** , the son of **Boaz** , the son of **Salmon** , the son of **Nahshon** , 33the son of **Amminadab** , the son of **Ram** , the son of **Hezron** , the son of **Perez** , the son of **Judah** , 34the son of **Jacob** , the son of **Isaac** , the son of **Abraham** , the son of **Terah** , the son of **Nahor** , 35the son of **Serug** , the son of **Reu** , the son of **Peleg** , the son of **Eber** , the son of **Shelah** , 36the son of **Cainan** , the son of **Arphaxad** , the son of **Shem** , the son of **Noah** , the son of **Lamech** , 37the son of **Methuselah** , the son of **Enoch** , the son of **Jared** , the son of **Mahalalel** , the son of **Cainan** , 38the son of **Enosh** , the son of **Seth** , the son of **Adam** , the son of **God** . Luke 3:23-38 ~ Mary's Lineage

In Luke's account, we read about Adam, the first man, who was the reason sin entered the world when he and his wife ate of the forbidden tree and disobeyed God.

On both the paternal and maternal lines of Jesus, we see Abraham, the man who lied about his wife being his sister because he lacked faith in God. He lied on two accounts to ensure he was not killed out of jealousy for his wife's beauty.

On both sides again, we trace Jesus' line to Rahab, a Canaanite woman who was also a harlot. She wasn't just an outsider/gentile; she practiced immorality so much that it became her identity.

Only a generation apart from Rahab, we see Ruth, a woman of Moab. The Moabites were not Israelites; instead, they descended from Lot, Abraham's nephew. Lot's wife died after looking back on the destruction of Sodom and Gomorrah. Lot's sons-in-law died within the city, never attempting to make it out, leaving only Lot and his two daughters. Both daughters, to keep their father's lineage alive, conceived a child with him: Moab and Ben-Ammi. So Ruth comes from a line that was started with incest, even from the culture's point of view during that time.

Two generations after that, we read about King David, the same man who committed adultery and then had the woman's husband murdered to cover it up.

In the next generation, we see Solomon, the man who had been led away from serving the LORD by his multitude of wives and their false idols.

Now, let me stop and say that I am being very negative and only pointing out the bad in these people's lives and the sins they've committed. That's not fair. So tell me, then, why do we, as people, only focus on the things we've done wrong when there's so much we have done right? Why is it so much easier to highlight not only ourselves but others' faults when imputing judgement?

Abraham, Rahab, and Ruth, though outsiders, also had great faith and respect for God throughout their lives. Romans 4 tells us that Abraham had unwavering faith. He was from Ur of the Chaldees, meaning that the promise to the Jews began with a gentile. And Rahab was more than an outsider and a harlot; she protected the spies who went into Jericho because she feared and respected God. Ruth, the Moabitess, clung to her mother-in-law, Naomi, and followed her back into the land of the Israelites, even though the land was foreign to her.

David and Solomon both had undeniable issues with the women in their lives, but David was still a man after God's own heart, following God, repenting of the evil he had committed, and living for God until the day of his death. Earlier in his life, David killed the Philistine giant who mocked his God. When David was on the run from Saul, he never harmed the LORD's anointed king of Israel, despite all the opportunities and reasons he had after Saul tried to murder him. And Solomon, early on, followed in his father's footsteps, building the temple that David so desperately wanted to build for the presence of God so that the LORD no longer had to dwell in a tent.

These people are more than their sins, and so are we. Even throughout our lives, though we will inevitably sin, God still has plans for us, plans of prosperity and hope for the future. We cannot be full of guilt and shame any longer; instead, we must give those things away unto our God who loves us.


---

## The Least in My Father's House
**Published:** 2026-04-30 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/04/the-least-in-my-fathers-house.html)**

---

One of my biggest struggles in life is pride. I catch myself asking questions like, “Why does my life not look like that person’s? As hard as I work, when do I get my opportunity?” And to be honest, this pride blinds me from reality.

Josiah Queen says it this way,

“The funny thing about pride is that you have it all your life, and the moment you think you don’t, it’s gotten in your eyes.”

“The funny thing about pride is that you have it all your life, and the moment you think you don’t, it’s gotten in your eyes.”

When I am blinded by pride, I fail to see the opportunities God has already allowed me. If you would allow me this moment, I want to praise God. I am so thankful for the opportunity to teach this class. I have the privilege of studying the Word of God and preparing weekly messages. I get to speak in front of all of you and sharpen my speech skills, and I know sometimes my fluency in English eludes me, which is why I'm thankful for each of you dedicating your time to the House of God on a Thursday night.

Another blessing that I have is my job. I am studying accounting at Eastern Illinois University, and I'm only a sophomore, so I haven't even gotten into the accounting courses yet. Despite that, I had the opportunity to work in First Neighbor's Tax and Accounting Department. It wasn't in the branch that I *wanted* , but God knew what I *needed* and put me in a place where the culture was relaxed and where I could take off work for personal reasons or for church.

I've said all of that to say that I am not special. Acts 10:34 tells us that God is no respecter of persons; if He did it for me, then He'll do it for you. Something I find so interesting about God is that He often chooses the least of the least to make a difference in His Kingdom.

I want to take this time to name a few people who would have likely called themselves the least of the least. These people didn't have the merits; they didn't have a college degree or the level of experience they could put on a résumé to get hired for a role. Well, if that's the case, then how do we know about the following individuals? That's because the Kingdom of God operates differently from this world.

It was Moses who said to God when being called to lead Israel out of slavery,

'"...Who am I that I should go to Pharaoh, and that I should bring the children of Israel out of Egypt?”' Exodus 3:11 NKJV

'"...Who am I that I should go to Pharaoh, and that I should bring the children of Israel out of Egypt?”' Exodus 3:11 NKJV

'..."O my Lord, I am not eloquent, neither before nor since You have spoken to Your servant; but I am slow of speech and slow of tongue.”' Exodus 4:10 NKJV

'..."O my Lord, I am not eloquent, neither before nor since You have spoken to Your servant; but I am slow of speech and slow of tongue.”' Exodus 4:10 NKJV

Gideon, when he was chosen to lead the Israelites into army, said,

'“...O my Lord, how can I save Israel? Indeed my clan is the weakest in Manasseh, and I am the least in my father’s house.”' Judges 6:15 NKJV

'“...O my Lord, how can I save Israel? Indeed my clan is the weakest in Manasseh, and I am the least in my father’s house.”' Judges 6:15 NKJV

King Saul, when he was chosen as the first King over Israel, said,

'“...Am I not a Benjamite, of the smallest of the tribes of Israel, and my family the least of all the families of the tribe of Benjamin? Why then do you speak like this to me?”' I Samuel 9:21 NKJV

'“...Am I not a Benjamite, of the smallest of the tribes of Israel, and my family the least of all the families of the tribe of Benjamin? Why then do you speak like this to me?”' I Samuel 9:21 NKJV

This next quote is interesting; it covers the life of David, but it isn't he who makes it; instead, it was David's father Jesse who doubted the ability of God's chosen king.

'And Samuel said to Jesse, “Are all the young men here?” Then he [Jesse] said, “There remains yet the youngest, and there he is, keeping the sheep...”' I Samuel 16:11 NKJV

'And Samuel said to Jesse, “Are all the young men here?” Then he [Jesse] said, “There remains yet the youngest, and there he is, keeping the sheep...”' I Samuel 16:11 NKJV

In all these cases, we aren't reading about people who are most apt for their role, at least not from a visual or logical standpoint, but God looks at the heart.

Earlier, I mentioned pride, which can be found in the heart, as well as its opposite: humility.

I love this quote, and it pretty well sums up humility,

“Humility is something that as soon as you think you have it, you've just lost it.”

“Humility is something that as soon as you think you have it, you've just lost it.”

To summarize this and the earlier quote, everyone has pride, especially if they won’t admit it; everyone has humility until they’ve admitted it.

The main point I want to drive home tonight is simply this: **be humble** .

Now, I'm not saying to beat yourself up and never celebrate successes in life; I believe that is part of the self-love the Bible teaches. What I am saying is that we are where we are today because of who our Father in Heaven is.

Philippians 4:13 says, ' **I can do all things through Christ who strengthens me** ' (NKJV). Okay, I want everyone to raise their hand if they believe God's Word is true...

Okay, now for that verse to be true, the inverse must also be true; we can do nothing without the strength of Christ... I tell you what, that very thought is humbling. One thing I want to put into practice: if you struggle with pride, or even if you don't, I would encourage this: understand that without God, we would still be dust of the earth.

We saw all that dust flying around outside last week; if it weren't for God, that would be us! We would have no control, we would have no consciousness. This leads to another thought– a freebie, as Brother Shawn would call it– because we have been given this control, we must understand that it is, firstly, a gift, so we should use it wisely, and secondly, that we are merely called to be stewards over said gift. God has not given us such an ability to waste it on worldly things– I'm not just speaking to all of you, I really needed to hear that tonight.

Let us end in prayer...


---

## One Thing
**Published:** 2026-04-16 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/04/one-thing.html)**

---

​I came across this passage in yesterday's Bible reading. I'm reading the Bible chronologically, and it is always interesting to see what is going on in David's life as he is writing the Psalms. He writes this in Psalm 27, verses 1-4:

'The Lord is my light and my salvation; Whom shall I fear? The Lord is the strength of my life; Of whom shall I be afraid? When the wicked came against me To eat up my flesh, My enemies and foes, They stumbled and fell. Though an army may encamp against me, My heart shall not fear; Though war may rise against me, In this I will be confident. **One thing I have desired of the Lord, That will I seek: That I may dwell in the house of the Lord All the days of my life, To behold the beauty of the Lord, And to inquire in His temple** .' Psalms 27:1-4 NKJV

'The Lord is my light and my salvation; Whom shall I fear? The Lord is the strength of my life; Of whom shall I be afraid? When the wicked came against me To eat up my flesh, My enemies and foes, They stumbled and fell. Though an army may encamp against me, My heart shall not fear; Though war may rise against me, In this I will be confident. **One thing I have desired of the Lord, That will I seek: That I may dwell in the house of the Lord All the days of my life, To behold the beauty of the Lord, And to inquire in His temple** .' Psalms 27:1-4 NKJV

At this time in David's life, he is fighting a Philistine army while on the run from Saul, the King of Israel, who is tormented by an evil spirit and trying to kill David for no righteous reason.

It would be understandable if David had just decided to lie down and cry out, "Woe is me– life is so difficult, I don't know if I can go on!" How many of us have been there? I was there just a few hours ago, struggling with a grade in one of my classes. Meanwhile, David is fighting a battle on two fronts, and instead says: "One thing I have desired of the LORD..."

If I'm David, there is a lot I desire from God in that moment. I'm thinking God, I want peace, I want rest, and I want confidence that all of this is going to be alright. And in a way, David does ask for all of these things, but only because he understood who his God was to him and who God will forever be. Because of this understanding, David has all of his desires met by God in one lump sum.

David said, "One thing I have desired of the Lord... That I may dwell in the house of the Lord All the days of my life..." I won't lie to you, and I'm sure many of you are already aware, but life can be so stressful. As the school year wraps up, I have to find time to study for upcoming finals while maintaining my responsibilities. I have to do x, y, and z. You all have a, b, and c in your lives. But throughout all of this stress, one thing that is so easily taken for granted is the presence of God.

You see, in the Bible days, if you wanted to find God, you had to go to the temple. Don't get me wrong: God was and is omnipresent, meaning He is everywhere at all times, but it wasn't the norm for just anyone to know God the way we know Him today. Not only was the temple in a very specific geographical location in the Middle East, but even if we were to travel there, we wouldn't be allowed in. Friends, you and I are what the Bible calls Gentiles. We wouldn't be allowed into the presence of God; we were considered heathens during that time of history!

Additionally, something I have discussed before and will continue to press is that if one wanted to talk to God, they had to go through a mediator, through a priest. So in that time of day, we would've had to have been in the Middle East, been Jewish, and hoped the priest was in session so we could talk to our Creator.

Thank God it's not that way today!

When Christ died on the Cross for our sins, the veil within the temple was torn, signifying that we no longer need a man to mediate between God and us– Jesus is our mediator so that we can talk directly to Him. In the Bible, Job speaks of a daysman and dreams of the day that we would have someone to go on our behalf before the LORD; yet here we are today taking for granted the ability we have been given to have such a closeness with God.

After Christ died, resurrected, and ascended into Heaven, He did not leave us *comfortless* but left us with the Comforter, found in His Spirit. That temple in the Middle East became a shadow, a metaphor for what Christ wants to do in us. God wants us to be His temple, holy and sanctified– separate from the world. We can do that by talking with Him.

Yesterday evening, I read this verse, but it wasn't until last night that I truly began to understand what was being laid on my heart. As many of you know, we had our Worship Night during our Wednesday night youth class– I hope you guys have been enjoying those as much as I have, because I have truly felt the presence of God move upon us. I became so reassured when I began talking to God and realized that no matter how our days are going or what we're going through, God is as close as the mention of His name.

I found myself repeating this throughout the night, and this is my prayer as we wrap up tonight, if you would, please stand... I found myself asking God, "LORD, help me grow close to you– let me bask in your presence." How many believe the LORD wants to grow closer to you? Raise your hand... Now, keep your hand up if you want to grow closer to God? It's a two-way street; it's a relationship. As we enter a time of prayer, let us have the same desire David had. More than anything in this world, the only thing He asked was that He might dwell within the presence of God. The presence of God is in this place, so let's pray and receive it...


---

## God’s Speed
**Published:** 2026-03-19 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/07/gods-speed-godspeed.html)**

---

Godspeed, as defined by Merriam-Webster's dictionary, means "A prosperous journey." It was used in the Middle Ages as a farewell, typically to wish someone good luck in a specific endeavor. To be quite frank, I had thought one used the word godspeed when telling someone to hurry up, although if that were the case, I feel as if those in my life would have used it more regarding my "fashionably late" lifestyle.

With that being said, I want to turn your attention to a different concept: God's speed.

“Lead me in Your truth and teach me, For You are the God of my salvation; On You I wait all the day.” Psalms 25:5 NKJV

When we are moving faster than something else, the things we pass become blurred (e.g., moving in a car).

Godly opportunities might become blurred; the inverse of that: stumbling blocks might become unblurred.

So how do you traverse life at the pace of God? You pray, daily, without ceasing (1 Thessalonians 5:17).

God's timing is perfect. When we think of the word godspeed, we often think of urgent, quick, to the point — but God's speed implies at His pace. I'd rather get to a destination on time and with God than early, or even late, and without Him.


---

## The Word of God
**Published:** 2026-03-12 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/03/the-word-of-god.html)**

---

Tonight, I want to discuss the Bible.

'All Scripture is given by inspiration of God, and is profitable for doctrine, for reproof, for correction, for instruction in righteousness, ' II Timothy 3:16 NKJV

'All Scripture is given by inspiration of God, and is profitable for doctrine, for reproof, for correction, for instruction in righteousness, ' II Timothy 3:16 NKJV

Now, when we say Scripture, we are referring to the Bible. There are a number of translations that the Bible has been written in. I predominantly read the King James Version, the KJV, in my personal devotion, but depending on the setting, I will use other translations when teaching. For instance, tonight I read from the New King James Version, the NKJV. For our campus Bible study at Eastern Illinois, we mostly use the English Standard Version because we have a number of ESV Bibles to hand out. On other occasions, I even use the Modern English Version, the MEV.

I have said all of that to say that, despite the number of translations, most of the time you'll find that each Bible contains the same 66 books. In youth class, you guys have discussed the 39 Old Testament books and the 27 New Testament books. I'm not sure if this language has been used by Bro. Tyler or Brody, but these 66 books are what we would classify as the canon (that's CANON, not CANNON). Simply put, Mr. Google would define the canon as "a collection or list of sacred books accepted as genuine." More than genuine, however, the Word of God is infallible, meaning it is incapable of being wrong. Another word you might hear to describe the Bible, which carries a little more weight, is "inerrant."

How I would distinguish the words infallible and inerrant is this: we believe that God spoke through the men who wrote the scriptures, and that God is incapable of being wrong; thus, the Bible is infallible. Some critics of the Bible might argue that while God doesn't make mistakes, mankind does, and therefore we cannot trust the Bible. That, however, is a false teaching. We believe that God not only had the power to give His Word to us, but also to keep and preserve it through these years.

It is fascinating, and often taken for granted, how well the Bible has been kept over the years. I don't know much detail about the timeline of its writings, frankly, that's something I should study. I do know this, though: the New Testament was written after the life of Jesus, which was 2000 years ago. Then there's the Old Testament, which was closed with the book of Malachi 400 years before the birth of Jesus, and we have to look even further back than that to see the majority of the Old Testament.

Much of the ancient world didn't read; it simply wasn't the norm like it is in the modern world. So, before Moses began writing the Pentateuch, the first five books of the Bible, everything had been orally passed down in the same manner we would remember a story, only that these weren't just stories; they were the tellings of Adam and Eve, Noah, Abraham, Issac, Jacob/Israel, and Joseph.

In the Bible, we see it referencing other writings from around that time, but this doesn't mean those other writings should be considered scripture. It is likely you will hear people refer to other books that we don't see in our Bible because we don't hold them to the same degree that the Canon is held.

There are a number of books considered canonical in certain denominations; the collection thereof is known as the Apocrypha. Once again, Mr. Google with a definition: "biblical or related writings not forming part of the accepted canon of Scripture."

The Apocryphal books are as follows:

1 Esdras

2 Esdras

Tobit

Judith

Additions to Esther

Wisdom of Solomon

Sirach (Ecclesiasticus)

Baruch

The Letter of Jeremiah

The Prayer of Azariah

Susanna

Bel and the Dragon

The Prayer of Manasseh

1 Maccabees

2 Maccabees

These are the books you would find included in a Catholic's Bible. What is fascinating is that the Jewish people preserved works such as 1 and 2 Maccabees, which describe the origin of Hanukkah, yet they don't consider them Scripture despite celebrating said holiday. Catholics *do* consider those books Scripture and *don't* celebrate Hanukkah.

Earlier, we mentioned the King James Bible– expanding on that, when the KJV was first printed, it included the apocryphal books.

As we wrap up tonight, I will say that I am not 100% familiar with the Apocrypha by any means. Some of what is read in the Apocrypha is historically accurate and true (lowercase t), but that does not make it True (capital T). I have heard the example that a math textbook is true, but it isn't scripture. Additionally, I don't believe that we need the Apocrypha because we have God's inerrant Word to teach us. I would, however, one day want to study the Apocrypha from a scholarly perspective, but I want to ensure I am rooted in the canonical Scripture, or God's Word, before taking that endeavor.


---

## The Glory of the LORD
**Published:** 2026-02-26 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/02/the-glory-of-lord.html)**

---

Before we start, let's pray.

Focus

Understanding

Application

One of the things I find much enjoyment in is listening to podcasts. I love being productive (I mean, who doesn't?), but I thrive on the cases of doing hard things in times when I don't want to. The type of podcasts I listen to is very edifying– some more than others, but one of the ones that I listen to is Brother Joel Urshan's, "The Fruitful Vine." Something that I have noticed when listening to this podcast is that at the end of every episode, he says, "May the LORD bless you and keep you, and may He cause His face to shine upon you." I was pretty familiar with this outro and didn't think much of it until I came across these words in my Bible reading. In Numbers 6, verses 24 through 27, God commands Moses as follows:

'“Speak to Aaron and his sons, saying, ‘This is the way you shall bless the children of Israel. Say to them: “The Lord bless you and keep you; The Lord make His face shine upon you, And be gracious to you; The Lord lift up His countenance upon you, And give you peace.”’' Numbers 6:23-26

'“Speak to Aaron and his sons, saying, ‘This is the way you shall bless the children of Israel. Say to them: “The Lord bless you and keep you; The Lord make His face shine upon you, And be gracious to you; The Lord lift up His countenance upon you, And give you peace.”’' Numbers 6:23-26

So, Moses is commanded to tell Aaron and the Levites, "This is how you should bless people. This is what you are to say." One of the things I want to do tonight is take a closer look at this blessing, which has multiple layers that all point to God's character.

Within the blessing, we see God's hold on the lives of His people, His provision in the words, "may He bless and keep you." Scripture tells us that we are "fearfully and wonderfully made (Psalm 139:14). We must understand that God has a plan for each and every one of us, and that we have a choice on whether or not we follow the Will of God.

We see the warmth and lovingkindness of God through the verse, "may His face shine upon you." In the beginning, God said, "Let there be light," but it is important to know that He did not create the sun, moon, or stars until the fourth day of creation. So where did this light come from? God was the light, and He is the light still today. I know the weather has been very indecisive (thank you, Illinois), but if you can just look back on a time in your life when it was actually nice outside, do you remember that feeling of warmth? There was not just warmth; creation thrived: leaves grew on trees and flowers bloomed. That's what it is like in the presence of God; we can thrive knowing He holds us close, and that even when we can't feel Him in certain seasons, as Job experienced, we have an understanding that He is as close as the mention of His name.

We see the judge that God is, when we read "may He be gracious unto you... and give you peace." Now, I know that grace and mercy are not the same thing; in fact, I read this noteworthy quote and wanted to share it real quick: Grace is getting something we do not deserve, but mercy is not getting something that we do deserve . How many have ever seen those shows on TV with the judges? I'm most familiar with "Judge Judy," and that might date me just a little. It was always entertaining to watch shows like that, wondering which call would be made. What was most surprising, however, was that there were times when, as a viewer, you would think, *Oh yeah, they're about to get it now* , and the judge would surprise you by showing mercy to the guilty. It can take us back and make us question how we treat others. *Would I have been as merciful* ? The same is true for God; He shows us mercy and grace every day, which can make us wonder if we are following in that same practice as Christians. We constantly fall short of the example God set for us when He walked this earth as a man, yet He still extends His hand toward us. He picks us up when we have fallen. We don't deserve it, but that's why it's called mercy.

Earlier, we read about the word "countenance," which means "face" or "presence". The verse says, "may the LORD lift up His countenance upon you." Now, let me preface by saying that God sees you. I know it is easy to think that God has overlooked you at times in your life, things just haven't seemed to go your way, but the LORD has ordered our steps and has us right where He wants us. So, although we might not feel that God has seen our face, He has, but we must also look toward His countenance. Now, I must admit I have never seen the face of God, but when it comes to His countenance, or in other words, His presence, I have seen it many times. We can feel the presence of God when we call upon Him in prayer, when we open our Bibles and read His Word, and even when we surround ourselves with other children of God, like we're doing right now.

In closing, I want to end on this thought. Moses was very close to God during his life, so close that while he was talking to God on Mount Sinai, writing down the Ten Commandments, God's face shone upon his own. When Moses came down, you could still see the glow on his face from his godly encounter (Exodus 34:29). When we spend time with God, it becomes very noticeable. The Bible says that Aaron and all the children of Israel were quick to notice Moses' new feature, to the point that Moses had to cover his face. But tonight I want to say that we mustn't cover our faces after being in fellowship with God. When people of the world see us, they should see the God we serve. So let his face shine brightly upon us so that our faces can light up the darkness that surrounds us in this world. Amen.


---

## A Holy Temple
**Published:** 2026-02-19 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/02/a-holy-temple.html)**

---

To start off tonight's discussion, I want to read one scripture from Leviticus 19:30.

'‘You shall keep My Sabbaths and reverence My sanctuary: I am the Lord.' Leviticus 19:30

'‘You shall keep My Sabbaths and reverence My sanctuary: I am the Lord.' Leviticus 19:30

This verse, coming from the book of Leviticus, is surrounded by many other verses like it. These verses contain the laws that were given to the Israelite people. Much of what Moses wrote (the first five books of the Old Testament) is often referred to as the Law of Moses because Moses was the prophet through whom God spoke at that time.

This law, in particular, that we just read about, said these children of God, whom Moses was speaking to, were to reverence God's sanctuary. The word "sanctuary" simply means a holy or sacred place. It's important to know that the kind of church we have today is not the same as the one they had in biblical times. Now, what do I mean by that? In the New Testament, people met for church in their homes. Different people would host, and people would gather together, as they would for a bible study in the modern era. In the Old Testament, these Levite priests served in the tabernacle, a portable tent they set up and took down as the Israelites wandered before entering the promised land.

That word reverence that we read, that word simply means to respect. It's interesting that, in the original Hebrew, the word for "fearing the LORD" is the same one used when we read about taking reverence for God's sanctuary. The word is yārē' (yaw-ray'), and it means to have a respect for.

What got me thinking about this topic was the idea that there are certain things we do at home that we wouldn't do at church. Now, I'm not strictly talking about sinful things; that's definitely a factor I want to discuss tonight, but another instance might be something silly: at home, I walk around barefoot– I'm talking toes out and all– but that's not something I would do at church. Another example: at home, I may eat in my room or the living room, but people don't typically eat in a church sanctuary because it could be disrespectful to that holy place. A quick note: some believers actually walk around barefoot at church because God told Moses to take off his shoes when he walked on holy ground. I don't know anyone personally who believes that, or at least I haven't seen anyone practice it, but I wanted to mention that it exists as a belief.

Back to the discussion at hand, there are certain things we do outside of church that we wouldn't necessarily do in church because they simply aren't the norm. When it comes to our lifestyles, we can probably all look back to a time when our church life didn't match the one at home. For instance, I could be a really loving saint at church, but then go home and get really angry at people when playing video games. Or I could come to church and hear a message that we should be merciful, but then go home and refuse to forgive my parents or siblings for something they did. First off, we are all guilty of something like this. I'll be the first to admit it. Second, we cannot be this double-minded; trying to live a double life will result in one of our lifestyles tripping up the other. The Psalmist David says he has never seen the righteous forsaken, so it's likely that lifestyle won't be the one that trips us up.

More importantly, what we need to talk about is what is considered God's sanctuary in modern-day society. At the time Moses wrote, the sanctuary was a portable tent; today, God's sanctuary remains portable, which means it isn't simply a church building. I'm thankful for a church building, and I'm thankful for buildings like the one we are in that allow us to gather, but God's design is much greater than that.

1 Corinthians 6:19 says this,

'Or do you not know that your body is the temple of the Holy Spirit who is in you, whom you have from God, and you are not your own? ' I Corinthians 6:19

'Or do you not know that your body is the temple of the Holy Spirit who is in you, whom you have from God, and you are not your own? ' I Corinthians 6:19

God wants to fill us with His Spirit; He wants to pour into us initially following the sign of speaking in other tongues. He then wants to continuously operate in our lives. God's Spirit doesn't stop at His first act of filling us; that's only the beginning.

I loved Bro. Tyler's message last night. If you weren't here or need a refresher, he talked about how God's Spirit is like water; it never runs out, and like a mighty river, it seeks control. God's Spirit can't have control in our lives unless we allow it, unless we wade into the water until it becomes too deep that we have to begin swimming toward where the Holy Ghost takes us.

Our bodies are the temple of God's Spirit; our bodies are something to be reverenced, to be respected. There are laws from the Old Testament that should still be followed today regarding how we treat our bodies, because they are a dwelling place for God. If we wouldn't graffiti a church building, we shouldn't mark our bodies. If we wouldn't put holes in a church, we shouldn't puncture holes in our bodies. We are fearfully and wonderfully made, the Bible says in Psalm 139:14, which uses the same Hebrew word, yārē, telling us that God has respect for us in the same manner that we are commanded to have for Him.

Additionally, we should respect our perceptions and senses. If we wouldn't watch or listen to something in this house, then we shouldn't be watching or listening to it at all, because we should show reverence for the holiness we carry. We were not only created by God, but once He fills us with His Spirit, God then lives inside us and operates through us.

I want to end in prayer, so that if there is anything we have been doing that does not demonstrate the holiness we are called to or glorify God as it should, we can repent and ask God to forgive us. Maybe, like me, you felt convicted after listening to Tyler's message last night and realized that you, too, have some wells that could be dug up, some flesh that is in the way of God's Spirit working in your life. Let's take this time and make room for God.


---

## Ministry
**Published:** 2026-02-12 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/02/ministry.html)**

---

The foundation of tonight's discussion comes from Ephesians 4, specifically in verse 11, which says,

'11And He Himself [this is Christ whom Paul is referring to] gave some to be apostles, some prophets, some evangelists, and some pastors and teachers...

'11And He Himself [this is Christ whom Paul is referring to] gave some to be apostles, some prophets, some evangelists, and some pastors and teachers...

Found in these verses, we read about the five-fold (sometimes called four-fold) ministry. We read about apostles, prophets, evangelists, pastors, and teachers. These are the ministerial office gifts; they are found elsewhere in the Bible, sometimes listed alongside the spiritual gifts, but they are still gifts.

We must ask ourselves this question: "Why did God give us these gifts?"

12for the equipping of the saints for the work of ministry, for the edifying of the body of Christ...

12for the equipping of the saints for the work of ministry, for the edifying of the body of Christ...

I want to hone in on that word "ministry." The Greek word used here is *diakonia* (dee-ak-on-ee'-ah), which is defined as "service, especially the kind in which a person is following the commandments of others." I like this distinction that Blue Letter Bible's lexicon gives; it makes sure we understand that ministry is not "service, specifically the act of serving oneself." If that were ministry, then everyone would be ministers, wouldn't they? Instead, ministry is serving others.

The Bible teaches that Jesus came to earth, *taking on the form of a servant* (Philippians 2:7). I have heard it said that Jesus did not come only to die. Jesus indeed came to die, that was His Plan, but understand two things tonight: number one, that Jesus did more than *simply* die, and number two, Jesus came to earth also to live. This life that Jesus lived exemplified how we should live, how we should act, and what it looks like to have a ministry. If Jesus came down only to die, He could've done it in a matter of moments rather than spending over thirty years with mankind. Jesus did it because He wanted a relationship– He wanted it then, and He wants it still today.

I'm so glad that Jesus came to live a life, because He now knows better than anyone that this flesh that each one of us has is difficult to deal with on our own, which is why Jesus is calling out to each of us, telling us we don't have to do this on our own.

Ministry is not something for a select few; the Bible says, "Many are called, but few are chosen" (Matthew 22:14). I think it can also be said that many are called, but few choose to accept that calling. Sometimes we overcomplicate what a ministry is. If we meet someone new at a place we're familiar with, we should introduce ourselves and show them around. The alternative is that they suffer from uncertainty, with the possibility of never returning and even refusing to leave their comfort zone again. Now, I know that's a bit dramatic, but all I'm saying is that the feelings they face can be avoided when we show love. Another instance: as Christians, we should be the type of people who, when we are walking through our daily lives, still take a moment to wave or smile at those we pass by. In both cases, we see acts of ministry.

You may be sitting here, and you're thinking, *Jon, I hear what you're saying, but like, actually, what is ministry?* Well, I'm glad you asked. Ministry is loving others. Many of you may be familiar with the verse in Matthew 28:37-40,

'Jesus said to him, “ ‘You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, with all your soul, and with all your mind.’ This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like it: ‘You shall love your neighbor as yourself.’ On these two commandments hang all the Law and the Prophets.”' Matthew 22:37-40

'Jesus said to him, “ ‘You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, with all your soul, and with all your mind.’ This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like it: ‘You shall love your neighbor as yourself.’ On these two commandments hang all the Law and the Prophets.”' Matthew 22:37-40

For context, Jesus is explaining to the scribes what the greatest commandment is. Given that Jesus is God manifested in the flesh, I'm going to go out on a limb here and say we can trust His character and credibility enough to say that these must, in fact, be the greatest commandments of all. Earlier, we said that ministry is defined as service, specifically regarding others' commands. Well, here we see the greatest commandment of all is to love others– the true definition of ministry.

The examples I gave of introducing ourselves to new people or being friendly are acts of ministry because they are expressions of love. Everyone can be loving, which means everyone can be a minister. As I mentioned earlier, we are all called to minister, and it's up to us whether we accept that calling.

Another commandment given by Jesus, often called the great commission (Matthew 28:18-20), is to spread the Gospel– teach people about what the Bible says and testify of all the ways God has changed your life.

We are called to minister; everyone here has a calling to build a ministry. It's just a matter of whether we accept that calling. I encourage everyone, myself included, that we don't just brush off this message as, "Yeah, I agree that we should love more," or "Yeah, we should spend more time seeking out our ministry," but instead that we would ask ourselves, "How can I minister today? How can I show the love of God to my family and friends right now?" As you're pondering that thought, let's take this time to pray and ask God to reveal it to us...


---

## Take Heed Lest Ye Fall
**Published:** 2026-01-29 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/01/the-bigger-they-are-harder-they-fall.html)**

---

Allow me to start tonight with an illustration; later, we will get into the scripture, but I want to create an understanding for all of us first.

I started a new job this year, but before that, I worked at Eastern Illinois University as the recycling student. My job was to drive around and pick up cardboard and paper from each building on campus. I had this big F-350 truck with a bed that raised up, so I could dump all the cardboard at a nearby recycling center. It didn't take long for me to find out that the fastest way to get the cardboard out of the truck was to raise the bed as steeply as I could get it without making contact with the garage ceiling, and then climb up the truck bed and slide down, pushing all of the cardboard down with me. For the most part, it was a great gig, but I fell so many times because cardboard isn't very sturdy, especially when the floor beneath it is angled at 45º to the ground. Even worse, when it got really cold, like it is now, and ice started to form in the truck bed covered in snow, I couldn't use my favorite method of clearing out the cardboard. However, every time I fell down, I thought of this piece of scripture found in 1 Corinthians 10:12,

'Therefore let him who thinks he stands take heed lest he fall.' I Corinthians 10:12 NKJV

'Therefore let him who thinks he stands take heed lest he fall.' I Corinthians 10:12 NKJV

If you have been part of or heard of the Twelve Steps here at Celebrate Recovery, you'd be familiar with Step Ten, which says, "We continue to take personal inventory and when we were wrong, promptly admitted it." The accompanying verse, which is one for each step, is the one we just read from 1 Corinthians. I think it is interesting the way the NIV Bible is translated for this verse when it says, ' **So, if you think you are standing firm, be careful that you don’t fall!** ' (1 Corinthians 10:12 NIV).

Do you know why I fell all those times in the back of that truck, or why a person might fall at all? It's because they *thought* they were standing firm. Notice how, on occasion, when we are walking somewhere, whether that be on a sidewalk or parking lot, we might get tripped up for a moment, but then are less likely to do it thereafter. Now, someone who is clumsy might trip every day, but I find when I stumble, that it comes in seasons, if you will. The same distinction can be made spiritually.

We use that language, tripping, stumbling, falling short, etc., to describe our walk with God. We must know that our relationship with God is a walk; we can't sprint and expect God to keep up with our pace. We also mustn't stop this walk when God has plans for us in His timing. However, no matter how hard we try, we are going to make mistakes, whether that be tripping, stumbling, or falling. We are humans, born into sin, and we are prone to sin. Now, that doesn't mean we should lean into sin just because it's naturally occurring. You know what else is naturally occurring– mold, thorns, toxins, parasites– all these things are natural, but that does not make them healthy for us. So how can we avoid sin when it is so abundant in the world today? We must find ourselves a firm foundation, one that even when we fall, it will catch us.

One thing that was great about the cardboard and paper I had to pick up was that they didn't hurt too badly when I fell on them; they were cushiony and broke my fall. Jesus is the same, God is the comforter, so that when we fall, we are not without comfort. 1 Corinthians 3:11 says,

'For no other foundation can anyone lay than that which is laid, which is Jesus Christ. ' I Corinthians 3:11 NKJV

'For no other foundation can anyone lay than that which is laid, which is Jesus Christ. ' I Corinthians 3:11 NKJV

We don't need any substitutes; we don't need to worry about creating any other foundation when we already have one in place, when we already have access to God, no matter where we are.

We must not be under the impression that we won't fall victim to the temptations of the world; as already stated, we will. It does not matter who we are or how we've been raised; the Enemy has plenty of traps set for us that will ensnare us from time to time. What matters is that we don't rely on ourselves for help; instead, we rely on God as our Helper. A firm foundation ensures that even when we fall *to* sin, we don't fall *into* sin.


---

## Conforming Culture
**Published:** 2026-01-22 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/01/conforming-culture.html)**

---

Today, I want to discuss culture, but first, allow me to define it. Per Webster's dictionary, culture is "the beliefs, customs, arts, etc. of a particular social group, place, or time." Culture can be seen in a variety of ways. How many love Mexican food? Chips and salsa, chicken and chorizo, a cold glass of horchata. What about Chinese food? Kung pao chicken, dumplings, fried rice, chow mein. I'm not trying to make anyone hungry (though I see that I failed with that. I could *really* use an horchata right about now). I'm just explaining that culture is everywhere.

Even stories are a form of culture. Today in one of my classes at Eastern, we talked about this dog that used to run around the EIU campus. His name was Napoleon, he was a stray mutt of the golden retriever variety. From 1945 to 1960, Napoleon wandered around the campus, attending football games, college graduations, and even class lectures, I mean this dog must've had like four degrees by the end of his life. Speaking of which, after the dog passed, they buried him at the castle on campus, and even recently dedicated a bench to him.

Another view of culture is this: You know when you're hanging out at a friend's house, and you both walk inside, and you wait to see what they do with their shoes, whether they keep them on or take them off at the front? That's an example of culture. In this case, one would typically conform and follow suit with whatever their friend does (it's their house after all). However, there are times when we shouldn't conform to the culture presented before us, when instead we should look to the Bible and offer a counter-cultural answer.

'1I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, that you present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable to God, which is your reasonable service. 2And do not be conformed to this world, but be transformed by the renewing of your mind, that you may prove what is that good and acceptable and perfect will of God.' Romans 12:1-2 NKJV

'1I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, that you present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable to God, which is your reasonable service. 2And do not be conformed to this world, but be transformed by the renewing of your mind, that you may prove what is that good and acceptable and perfect will of God.' Romans 12:1-2 NKJV

Paul writes that we should not conform to the ideas of this world or to beliefs contrary to God's Holy Word. Living a life of holiness means living a life of separation, setting us apart from the world. However, living for God does not mean putting walls up toward non-believers. The Bible tells us in John 3:16 that, '... **God so loved the world that He gave His only begotten Son, that whoever believes in Him should not perish but have everlasting life** .' John 3:16 NKJV

Jesus loved the world– our Creator and our Savior. He loved the world. Jesus was the example of how to live, meaning that because He loved the world, so should we. The distinction here for loving the world is that the word "world" used here refers to mankind. Another piece of scripture, also written by the Apostle John, says:

'Do not love the world or the things in the world. If anyone loves the world, the love of the Father is not in him. For all that is in the world—the lust of the flesh, the lust of the eyes, and the pride of life—is not of the Father but is of the world. And the world is passing away, and the lust of it; but he who does the will of God abides forever.' I John 2:15-17 NKJV

'Do not love the world or the things in the world. If anyone loves the world, the love of the Father is not in him. For all that is in the world—the lust of the flesh, the lust of the eyes, and the pride of life—is not of the Father but is of the world. And the world is passing away, and the lust of it; but he who does the will of God abides forever.' I John 2:15-17 NKJV

We know that the Word of God does not contradict itself; it is infallible, incapable of being wrong. The Bible is inspired by God; it says in John 1:1 that the Word *was* God. Is our God wrong? No, which means neither is His Word. So why does the Bible say that God loved the world, yet instructs us not to love the world? The part of the world we are supposed to love is the people; it's mankind. The rest of the world is not meant to be loved or sought after. We care for people because they are God's creation, but money, power, and glory, all of those things were created by the world and are not meant for us.

There is a passage of scripture that makes it clear that the culture had been changed by the people of God.

'But the Jews who were not persuaded, becoming envious, took some of the evil men from the marketplace, and gathering a mob, set all the city in an uproar and attacked the house of Jason, and sought to bring them out to the people. But when they did not find them, they dragged Jason and some brethren to the rulers of the city, crying out, “These who have turned the world upside down have come here too. ' Acts 17:5-6 NKJV

'But the Jews who were not persuaded, becoming envious, took some of the evil men from the marketplace, and gathering a mob, set all the city in an uproar and attacked the house of Jason, and sought to bring them out to the people. But when they did not find them, they dragged Jason and some brethren to the rulers of the city, crying out, “These who have turned the world upside down have come here too. ' Acts 17:5-6 NKJV

There were devout Jews, those who practiced the Old Testament ways but refused to accept Jesus as their savior and remained ensnared in the Old Covenant. They hated what the followers of Jesus were doing to the culture, so much so that they ransacked Jason's house and dragged him before the rulers of the city.

When I was in high school, I went to a leadership development center as part of my coursework. They taught us several things, but what I want to leave with all of you is this illustration. You have a carrot, an egg, and a coffee bean. If you put each of those into a boiling pot of water, something changes. The boiling pot of water signifies the environment that we live in–– which is poetic given that we as Christians live in a world that hates us (John 15:18-19).

When you place the carrot in this environment, it softens and weakens. In the life of a believer, this might look like somebody who has forsaken the Truth that they have been brought up in for that which is enticing and of the world.

When you place the egg into the boiling environment, it hardens. This is very similar to a believer who puts up walls, keeping every non-believer out of their life, making assumptions and judgments compared to that of their own righteous living.

However, unlike the former two scenarios, the coffee bean has a different effect. When a coffee bean is placed in a boiling pot of water, the environment begins to change; it is no longer water, but coffee. This is the path that we as Christians should choose, striving to change the culture rather than let it conform us. We should be loving people just as Christ demonstrated for us. We must turn the world, turn the culture upside down, showing people the goodness that is found in Christ Jesus.

References

'“18If the world hates you, you know that it hated Me before it hated you. 19If you were of the world, the world would love its own. Yet because you are not of the world, but I chose you out of the world, therefore the world hates you.' John 15:18-19 NKJV

'“18If the world hates you, you know that it hated Me before it hated you. 19If you were of the world, the world would love its own. Yet because you are not of the world, but I chose you out of the world, therefore the world hates you.' John 15:18-19 NKJV

## God is Greater Than the New Year
**Published:** 2026-01-15 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2026/01/god-is-greater-than-new-year.html)**

---

January 9th, 2026, was the second Friday in January, otherwise known as Quitter's Day. In our culture, we greatly prize the New Year and the resolutions that come with it. We share our resolutions and listen to others', and there's nothing wrong with doing either. However, what I want to focus on is that those resolutions don't always work out, and statistically, most people give up on them on the second Friday of January every year. People have already begun to realize that the expectations they set for themselves were too high, or that they didn't have as much time or willpower as they had hoped they would coming into this New Year.

This all got me thinking: why do we, as a society, wait so long to make changes in our lives? When our resolutions don't turn out, we say, "I'll just try it again next year." I recently heard a preacher say that we give too much power to the New Year, thinking that we cannot be changed until it starts back up again. While I made my own resolutions, I feel the need to point out a couple of flaws. If we wait every year until January 1st to be better, jumping into a whole new year full of obstacles, it's no surprise that we struggle and "fail" when the unexpected comes our way. Another point I want to make: just because we missed a day on one of our resolutions does not mean we have failed it. Being in school, I know that just because I missed 1/365 points available, or even 1/260 (when counting only weekdays), I don't automatically have a failing grade. Habits are hard to create; there will be setbacks. What matters is how we approach those setbacks.

What I want to focus most on in this discussion is that the New Year has no power over any of us. If we want to be made new, completely and utterly changed, then we need to give our attention to the One who truly holds that kind of power; we need to look toward Jesus.

'Therefore, if anyone is in Christ, he is a new creation; old things have passed away; behold, all things have become new.' II Corinthians 5:17

'Therefore, if anyone is in Christ, he is a new creation; old things have passed away; behold, all things have become new.' II Corinthians 5:17

When we are in Jesus, we are born anew. The concept of being born again isn't just something that church leaders say to increase attendance; it's biblical. In John 3, Jesus is talking with Nicodemus and tells him, " **Most assuredly, I say to you, unless one is born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God** " (John 3:3). Nicodemus, as the modern youths would say, was flabbergasted. He replied, " **How can a man be born when he is old? Can he enter a second time into his mother's womb and be born?** " (John 3:4). Jesus simplifies it for him and us as modern-day readers:

'Jesus answered, “Most assuredly, I say to you, unless one is born of water and the Spirit, he cannot enter the kingdom of God.' John 3:5

'Jesus answered, “Most assuredly, I say to you, unless one is born of water and the Spirit, he cannot enter the kingdom of God.' John 3:5

If we want to be new creatures in Christ and experience the new birth, then we must be baptized by water and filled with the Spirit. I recently heard it said that when we are baptized by water in Jesus name, we are in Him. Paul asks in his writings,

'Know ye not, that so many of us as were baptized into Jesus Christ were baptized into his death? Therefore we are buried with him by baptism into death: that like as Christ was raised up from the dead by the glory of the Father, even so we also should walk in newness of life. ' Romans 6:3-4 KJV

'Know ye not, that so many of us as were baptized into Jesus Christ were baptized into his death? Therefore we are buried with him by baptism into death: that like as Christ was raised up from the dead by the glory of the Father, even so we also should walk in newness of life. ' Romans 6:3-4 KJV

However, when we are filled with God's Spirit, Jesus dwells in us. Paul asks a different question:

'What? know ye not that your body is the temple of the Holy Ghost which is in you, which ye have of God, and ye are not your own? For ye are bought with a price: therefore glorify God in your body, and in your spirit, which are God's.' 1 Corinthians 6:19-20 KJV

'What? know ye not that your body is the temple of the Holy Ghost which is in you, which ye have of God, and ye are not your own? For ye are bought with a price: therefore glorify God in your body, and in your spirit, which are God's.' 1 Corinthians 6:19-20 KJV

Baptism and the filling of the Spirit are two of the three components in justification, the steps that each believer must take at the beginning of their walk with God. The order of occurrence in these two acts is interchangeable, though we must understand that receiving the gift of God's Spirit comes from an act of Him offering said gift. I will say that God wants to fill everyone with His Spirit, ' **For the promise is unto you, and to your children, and to all that are afar off, even as many as the Lord our God shall call** ' (Acts 2:39).

Before focusing too much on baptism and the infilling of God's Spirit, a person must understand repentance. We all must repent of the wrongs we have committed, even after God dwells inside us. We are all descendants of Adam and have what is denoted as an Adamic nature, meaning we are prone to sin just as the first man, Adam, was. While sin is an inevitable part of our lives, God made a way for us to be forgiven of our sins. We are no longer under the Old Testament covenant. Because of Jesus' sacrifice on the cross, we get to come to Him in prayer and ask for forgiveness for the things we've done. An act of repentance means turning 180º, turning one's life around, and going in the other direction towards the things of God. In a new year, everyone wants a fresh start, a new beginning. We can have that through Christ.

I have heard it said many times, and it is such a paradox, but that doesn't make it any less true; when we need a change in our lives, let us go to the God who never changes, " **Jesus Christ the same yesterday, and to day, and for ever** " ** ** (Hebrews 13:8). If you've let go of your New Year's resolutions, worry not, because a walk with God can be picked up at any point in the year and will change your life forever.

References

'Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, ' Matthew 28:19 NKJV '38Then Peter said to them, “Repent, and let every one of you be baptized in the name of Jesus Christ for the remission of sins; and you shall receive the gift of the Holy Spirit. 39For the promise is to you and to your children, and to all who are afar off, as many as the Lord our God will call.”' Acts 2:38-39 NKJV

'Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, ' Matthew 28:19 NKJV

'38Then Peter said to them, “Repent, and let every one of you be baptized in the name of Jesus Christ for the remission of sins; and you shall receive the gift of the Holy Spirit. 39For the promise is to you and to your children, and to all who are afar off, as many as the Lord our God will call.”' Acts 2:38-39 NKJV

## Change
**Published:** 2025-12-11 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/12/change.html)**

---

As we gear up for the coming of a new year, I feel that it is fitting to talk about change. Of course, I must acknowledge the fact that, generally speaking, we as people are uncomfortable with change. I questioned a few of my friends and their thoughts on the subject, and we all came to the belief that change is exciting yet scary. Change comes with uncertainty, and it is often said that the only thing a person can be certain of is that change will take place in their life.

We can see change even in nature, in the transitioning of the seasons. At one time, I dressed for work in a t-shirt and jeans, but now I find myself wearing a thermal shirt under another long-sleeve shirt and a hoodie. One may reasonably argue that I am what most would call a "baby" when it comes to cold weather. While personally annoyed by the cooler temperatures, I look forward to the coming year and the change that awaits me. I will start a new semester of college with new classes and professors, as well as a new job. It is because of this level of change in my life that I wanted to speak about it.

Throughout this discussion, let's ask ourselves this question: How do we prepare for change when it carries so much uncertainty?

I have heard one of my church leaders say, "If you need a change in your life, can I introduce you to Jesus Christ?" I love the fact that when life is falling apart, we can lean on our Strongtower for support. However, I have to speak on the paradox that presents itself here; when we need a change in our lives, we require a God who never changes.

'Jesus Christ is the same yesterday, today, and forever. ' Hebrews 13:8

'Jesus Christ is the same yesterday, today, and forever. ' Hebrews 13:8

The consistency of God is one that every Christian needs to understand. The God who loved His people in the Old Testament, and loved us enough to come down as a man and die for us, is the same God who loves us today. We have all made mistakes, and we will create even more as life goes on, but nothing we do will change the love that God has for us.

Not only is God's love consistent, but so is the fulfillment of His promises. We can read about Abraham's promise that he would be the father of many nations. Abraham and his wife were old, and she was barren, but God's promise came to pass nonetheless when they had their promised son, Issac. Out of Issac comes Jacob, who would later be named Israel. Out of Israel come the 12 Tribes of Israel, including Judah, out of which comes the King of the Jews, Jesus Christ Himself.

Abraham and Sarah doubted God's promise, which is why Ishmael came before Issac when Abraham and Sarah tried to force the promise to come to pass. I'm sure even after the fact, Abraham never realized the full extent of God's promise that out of Abraham's lineage God would manifest Himself as Christ Jesus. I have this confidence because no one can grasp the degree of God's power and reason, but know that when God makes a promise to us, He is faithful and things will come to pass that we can't even imagine.

For God's promises to come to pass, we must recognize His power. God is the beginning and the end. The first letter in the Greek alphabet is Alpha, the last letter is Omega. When we say God is Alpha and Omega, we mean He is the beginning and the end.

If you are anything like my sister and me, you often argue with your siblings. One of my common pastimes is ragebaiting her (I will be the first to say this is not biblical). We do this often enough that our mom will break it up, and my sister will typically say, "He started it," to which my mom replies, "Well, I'm finishing it!"

I tell that story to say that God is the starter and the finisher. Now, I must quickly state that while I pick fights with my 13-year-old sister, God works all things for good (Romans 8:28). Going back to God's promises, we said that God is faithful and that if He promises us something, He will perform it. Why is that? It's because God is the starter and the finisher, the beginning and the end, the Alpha and Omega. Before there was existence, there was God. Before there was light, God was the light. Before there was man, there was God, who loved man and had a plan to save mankind.

God is omnipotent; He is all-powerful, infinitely-powerful. Looking at some Greek myths, we can read about beings who supposedly hold all this power, yet they can be defeated. The Titans were defeated by the Olympian gods (make note of the lower-case "g"), and before that, Kronos had swallowed a majority of the Olympians, defeating them. However, the God you and I serve does not have those kinds of limitations, or any limits for that matter. In the creation of these mythological beings, the Greek gods came from the Titans, and the Titans from the Primordials, but God (capital "G") exists on His own; He has no predecessor.

When we find ourselves overwhelmed with change, let us go to the God who never changes. Let us go to the God who has never stopped loving us and never will. Let us go to the God who has always been in control, existing even before time itself. Let us trust in the consistency of our Savior Jesus Christ, who " **is the same yesterday, today, and forever.** "

References

'And we know that all things work together for good to those who love God, to those who are the called according to His purpose.' Romans 8:28 NKJV

'And we know that all things work together for good to those who love God, to those who are the called according to His purpose.' Romans 8:28 NKJV

## The Dangers of Being Double-Minded
**Published:** 2025-12-04 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/12/the-dangers-of-being-double-minded.html)**

---

Jesus Christ was dual-natured, 100% man and 100% God. But despite this duality, He is still one God. Like the oneness of God, we all must be in one mind and one accord. It was only after everyone was of one mind and in one accord that they were filled with the initial outpouring of the Holy Spirit on the day of Pentecost (Acts 2). This alone demonstrates the significance of being united with our brothers and sisters in Christ. We should all have the same overarching mission– the one given to us by Jesus when he says,

'Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age.” Amen.' Matthew 28:19-20 NKJV

'Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age.” Amen.' Matthew 28:19-20 NKJV

Known as the Great Commission, this call from our LORD and Savior, Jesus Christ, is to spread the Truth of His Word and make disciples. While we are not one of the original disciples, we are all still disciples of Christ. In 1 Corinthians 11:1, Paul says to follow him (or "imitate him" as translated by the NKJV) as he follows Christ. Paul was a disciple, not one of the original disciples, but still a disciple of Christ who converted others to discipleship. Looking at the function of a pastor in a church, we follow our pastors because, like Paul, they follow Christ, and in turn, we then follow Christ. We are disciples and are called to lead others to God.

The main point I want to make in this discussion is that we cannot follow the Bible's instructions for following Jesus if we do not begin by serving God and God alone. Scripture tells us that He is a jealous God (Exodus 20:5). He wants our entire focus and dedication, not one split between His Will and our own. This is an example of double-mindedness. A double mind is living for God on a Sunday, or even a Wednesday, all while feeding our flesh the rest of the week with the desires that we have. A double mind means knowing what is right and choosing to do the contrary. The book of 1 John warns us that if we claim to know God and yet choose not to follow his commandments, we are lying to ourselves. We cannot be truthful people if we are living contrary to *the* Truth, which means we are liars until we find ourselves in the complete and utter Truth of God's Word.

The book of James warns us that we cannot have a double mind:

'If any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask of God, who gives to all liberally and without reproach, and it will be given to him. But let him ask in faith, with no doubting, for he who doubts is like a wave of the sea driven and tossed by the wind. For let not that man suppose that he will receive anything from the Lord; he is a double-minded man, unstable in all his ways.' James 1:5-8 NKJV

'If any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask of God, who gives to all liberally and without reproach, and it will be given to him. But let him ask in faith, with no doubting, for he who doubts is like a wave of the sea driven and tossed by the wind. For let not that man suppose that he will receive anything from the Lord; he is a double-minded man, unstable in all his ways.' James 1:5-8 NKJV

Paul also uses an analogy about being tossed by the winds of false doctrine, or false teaching (Ephesians 4:14). There is a false belief that we, as God's children, can do whatever we want in life so long as we "just believe." Belief is not a matter of acknowledging God's existence; even agnostics recognize a higher power in life. Believing in God is following His commandments. This misunderstanding is likely derived from Acts 16:31. Allow me to paint the picture.

Paul and Silas have just been freed from prison through the supernatural deliverance of an earthquake. Their jailer is about to take his own life for fear that he will lose it to his superior, punishing him for allowing the prisoners to escape. Keep in mind, Paul and Silas are thrown in jail for preaching the Gospel; this man's superior was not a kind man, and the jailer, in my understanding, had every right to be scared. However, before the jailer harms himself, Paul calls out to him, informing him that they had not tried to escape. The jailer then asks the two men, ' **Sirs, what must I do to be saved?** ' and in verse 31 they reply, ' **Believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, and you will be saved, you and your household.** ' Now, hear me when I say that the story goes on. In the coming verses, this man and his household are baptized as a result of their belief. It is not as simple as acknowledging the existence of God and that the Bible is true, but believing is living out the Truth that the Bible teaches and giving ourselves entirely over to Christ through repentance and the crucifying of our flesh as we "die daily" (1 Corinthians 15:31).

We cannot be double-minded and try to serve ourselves while serving God; we have to be all in on Christ. The Bible tells us that we cannot serve two masters (Matthew 6:24); it just doesn't work. It's not an act of true servitude if our focus is divided. When I think of a double-minded person, I think of a person who is trying to live two lives. I have heard it said that, "When you're two people at the same time, one of them is bound to trip the other." If we try to live two lives, we will find ourselves falling on our faces at some point. The verse we read earlier from James says a double-minded person is unstable in all of their ways, and this is why. On the day of Pentecost, their focus was unified; they all wanted more of their Creator, and they got it through the infilling of the Holy Ghost, a promise that still exists today. That's the promise, beyond the initial pouring out of God's Spirit, we can walk with Him and hear His voice if we would just begin to dedicate our lives to Him. I am well aware that a life of sacrifice is a heavy price, but He paid an even greater price on the Cross, and I promise you that you won't regret living wholly for God.

The Psalmist of Psalm 119:113 goes as far as to say, ' **I hate the double-minded, But I love Your law.** ' They said this because, just like John, they knew that the Word of God is so important. John writes in his Gospel account, John 1:1, ' **In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God.** ' Later, in verse 14, John writes, ' **And the Word became flesh and dwelt among us, and we beheld His glory, the glory as of the only begotten of the Father, full of grace and truth.** '

If today we find ourselves struggling to be of one mind, to carry out the mind of Christ (1 Corinthians 2:16), to carry out the Will of God for our lives, then we must be reading the Scriptures. To be singularly minded, focused on the things of God, then we must get to know Him through the reading of His Word. Like the psalmist, we must love His law and keep His commandments; only then can we find ourselves living with a single mindset, one that glorifies Him and advances His Kingdom. Let's be praying that God will help us to have a single mindset and a love for Truth.
