## Jehovah
**Published:** 2025-11-13 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/11/jehovah.html)**

---

In this discussion, I want to study the name of Jehovah. I will start off by answering these three questions: 1) Who is Jehovah? 2) Where does the name Jehovah come from? and 3) Why does this information matter?

**Who** is Jehovah?

'And God spake unto Moses, and said unto him, I am the LORD: and I appeared unto Abraham, unto Isaac, and unto Jacob, by the name of God Almighty, but by my name JEHOVAH was I not known to them. ' Exodus 6:2-3 KJV

'And God spake unto Moses, and said unto him, I am the LORD: and I appeared unto Abraham, unto Isaac, and unto Jacob, by the name of God Almighty, but by my name JEHOVAH was I not known to them. ' Exodus 6:2-3 KJV

I have discussed the significance of LORD with all caps in instances such as this; it was used to distinguish it from the word "lord," meaning someone of high position, who was not *the* LORD God. JEHOVAH has been used in this manner as well; both cases refer to YHWH. Even in writing the name, the Jewish people did not want people to lose reverence for God's name or to mistake it for anything less than sacred, so they shortened it to these four letters, which we call the Tetragrammaton, so that the exact spelling and translation would remain unknown.

**Where** is Jehovah Derived?

Jehovah, or *yᵊhōvâ* (yeh-ho-vaw') in its Hebrew equivalence, means "the existing One." It is the proper name for God in the Old Testament, but it is an Anglicized form, meaning it was borrowed into English. Simply put, Jehovah is the English form of YHWH.

**Why** Does it Matter?

So here we are, I have given a little context and laid some foundation, but you're probably asking, "Jon, what's the point here? Why should any of this be significant to us?" Well, I'm glad you asked. Bear with me for just a few more moments, and this will all come together. The Bible, comprising both the Old and New Testaments, is the inspired Word of God (2 Timothy 3:16), so we cannot single out just one testament or book; we must honor the entirety of God's Word.

Looking at the word "adonai" in its Hebrew form, we get ' *āḏôn* (aw-done'), which means "lord," – not *the* LORD, but lord as in master. Earlier, we read a verse in Exodus in which God tells Moses that Abraham did not know the name of the LORD, the name of God. We see this to be true in the following verse:

'After these things the word of the LORD came unto Abram in a vision, saying, Fear not, Abram: I am thy shield, and thy exceeding great reward. And Abram said, **Lord GOD** , what wilt thou give me, seeing I go childless, and the steward of my house is this Eliezer of Damascus?' Genesis 15:1-2 KJV

'After these things the word of the LORD came unto Abram in a vision, saying, Fear not, Abram: I am thy shield, and thy exceeding great reward. And Abram said, **Lord GOD** , what wilt thou give me, seeing I go childless, and the steward of my house is this Eliezer of Damascus?' Genesis 15:1-2 KJV

I want to focus on Abraham's response when he says, "Lord GOD..." Lord, in this context, is the Hebrew word ' *ăḏōnāy* (ad-o-noy'), which is just a variation of *āḏôn* . Abraham called God his lord, but he did not know God as Moses would. While Abraham called God Adonai, Moses was able to call Him YHWH. Furthermore, I would like to say that Moses did not know God as we do today. Just like Abraham was limited in seeing God as YHWH, Moses did not yet see God as Jesus. This is a type, or shadow, for us New Testament believers: the God we serve has been called many names and holds many titles, but we learn in the Gospel that the LORD our God's name is Jesus Christ.

Hebrews 13:8 tells us that Jesus is the same yesterday, today, and forever. This means that all the traits Jehovah held, Jesus carries too, because Jehovah of the Old Testament is the same Jesus Christ of the New Testament.

Some of those titles include:

Jehovah-Jireh, "The LORD will provide"

'And Abraham lifted up his eyes, and looked, and behold behind him a ram caught in a thicket by his horns: and Abraham went and took the ram, and offered him up for a burnt offering in the stead of his son. And Abraham called the name of that place **Jehovah-jireh** : as it is said to this day, In the mount of the LORD it shall be seen. ' Genesis 22:13-14 KJV

'And Abraham lifted up his eyes, and looked, and behold behind him a ram caught in a thicket by his horns: and Abraham went and took the ram, and offered him up for a burnt offering in the stead of his son. And Abraham called the name of that place **Jehovah-jireh** : as it is said to this day, In the mount of the LORD it shall be seen. ' Genesis 22:13-14 KJV

Jesus provides for us still today. He instructs His disciples to pray daily and ask Him to provide for their needs, "Give us this day our daily bread" (Matthew 6:11).

Jehovah-Rapha, "The LORD who heals"

'And the people murmured against Moses, saying, What shall we drink? And he cried unto the LORD; and the LORD shewed him a tree, which when he had cast into the waters, the waters were made sweet: there he made for them a statute and an ordinance, and there he proved them, and said, If thou wilt diligently hearken to the voice of the LORD thy God, and wilt do that which is right in his sight, and wilt give ear to his commandments, and keep all his statutes, I will put none of these diseases upon thee, which I have brought upon the Egyptians: for **I am the LORD that healeth thee** .' Exodus 15:24-26

'And the people murmured against Moses, saying, What shall we drink? And he cried unto the LORD; and the LORD shewed him a tree, which when he had cast into the waters, the waters were made sweet: there he made for them a statute and an ordinance, and there he proved them, and said, If thou wilt diligently hearken to the voice of the LORD thy God, and wilt do that which is right in his sight, and wilt give ear to his commandments, and keep all his statutes, I will put none of these diseases upon thee, which I have brought upon the Egyptians: for **I am the LORD that healeth thee** .' Exodus 15:24-26

Jesus gave the blind their sight. He told the lame man to take up his bed and walk. He healed the woman with the issue of blood. He raised Lazarus from the dead. That just names a few of the miracles recorded in the Gospel, and even then, the Bible tells us that not every miracle was recorded (John 20:30-31, John 21:25).

Jehovah-Nissi, "The LORD is my banner"

'And the LORD said unto Moses, Write this for a memorial in a book, and rehearse it in the ears of Joshua: for I will utterly put out the remembrance of Amalek from under heaven. And Moses built an altar, and called the name of it **Jehovah-nissi** : for he said, Because the LORD hath sworn that the LORD will have war with Amalek from generation to generation.' Exodus 17:14-16 KJV

'And the LORD said unto Moses, Write this for a memorial in a book, and rehearse it in the ears of Joshua: for I will utterly put out the remembrance of Amalek from under heaven. And Moses built an altar, and called the name of it **Jehovah-nissi** : for he said, Because the LORD hath sworn that the LORD will have war with Amalek from generation to generation.' Exodus 17:14-16 KJV

Jesus is the God who fights for us. He went to the Cross so that we could be free. If Jesus were not God, that would imply that Jehovah sent someone else to fight for us. Pastor Joel Urshan said it this way, "[Our] God does not delegate," meaning the God we serve did not send someone else to bear the weight of sin; instead, He came down as a man so that we could have a high priest who better knew our shortcomings (Hebrews 4:15).

Jehovah-Mekoddishkem, "The LORD who sanctifies you"

'And the LORD spake unto Moses, saying, Speak thou also unto the children of Israel, saying, Verily my sabbaths ye shall keep: for it is a sign between me and you throughout your generations; that ye may know that **I am the LORD that doth sanctify you** .' Exodus 31:12-13

'And the LORD spake unto Moses, saying, Speak thou also unto the children of Israel, saying, Verily my sabbaths ye shall keep: for it is a sign between me and you throughout your generations; that ye may know that **I am the LORD that doth sanctify you** .' Exodus 31:12-13

I have taught before how God created us in His image and how He sanctifies us so that His image can be preserved in us. He has called us to be " **a chosen generation, a royal priesthood, an holy nation, a peculiar people** " (1 Peter 2:9) so that we may be different than those in the world, so that we may shed light within the darkness.

Jehovah-Shalom, "The LORD is peace"

'And when Gideon perceived that he was an angel of the LORD, Gideon said, Alas, O Lord GOD! for because I have seen an angel of the LORD face to face. And the LORD said unto him, Peace be unto thee; fear not: thou shalt not die. Then Gideon built an altar there unto the LORD, and called it **Jehovah-shalom** : unto this day it is yet in Ophrah of the Abi-ezrites.' Judges 6:22-24 KJV

'And when Gideon perceived that he was an angel of the LORD, Gideon said, Alas, O Lord GOD! for because I have seen an angel of the LORD face to face. And the LORD said unto him, Peace be unto thee; fear not: thou shalt not die. Then Gideon built an altar there unto the LORD, and called it **Jehovah-shalom** : unto this day it is yet in Ophrah of the Abi-ezrites.' Judges 6:22-24 KJV

I have been taught that if we want to walk in the LORD's Will, we must walk toward peace. The peace that the LORD brings into our lives is like none other. I was diagnosed with anxiety when I was 15 years old, but once I found myself grounded in church and in the Word of God, the anxiety disappeared over time, and the LORD became my peace.

Jehovah-Tsidkenu, "The LORD our righteousness"

'Behold, the days come, saith the LORD, that I will raise unto David a righteous Branch, and a King shall reign and prosper, and shall execute judgment and justice in the earth. In his days Judah shall be saved, and Israel shall dwell safely: and this is his name whereby he shall be called, **THE Lord OUR RIGHTEOUSNESS** . ' Jeremiah 23:5-6 KJV

'Behold, the days come, saith the LORD, that I will raise unto David a righteous Branch, and a King shall reign and prosper, and shall execute judgment and justice in the earth. In his days Judah shall be saved, and Israel shall dwell safely: and this is his name whereby he shall be called, **THE Lord OUR RIGHTEOUSNESS** . ' Jeremiah 23:5-6 KJV

Reading and interpreting this verse made me emotional. I am not trying to overexaggerate or generate something that isn't there, but I felt as if I was discovering the Oneness of God for the first time. Jehovah-Tsidkenu is a title given unto Jehovah, but this verse from Jeremiah is a prophecy of Jesus' coming to earth. Jesus is Jehovah.

Jehovah-Shammah, "The LORD is there"

'And these are the goings out of the city on the north side, four thousand and five hundred measures. And the gates of the city shall be after the names of the tribes of Israel: three gates northward; one gate of Reuben, one gate of Judah, one gate of Levi. And at the east side four thousand and five hundred: and three gates; and one gate of Joseph, one gate of Benjamin, one gate of Dan. And at the south side four thousand and five hundred measures: and three gates; one gate of Simeon, one gate of Issachar, one gate of Zebulun. At the west side four thousand and five hundred, with their three gates; one gate of Gad, one gate of Asher, one gate of Naphtali. It was round about eighteen thousand measures: and the name of the city from that day shall be, **The LORD is there** .' Ezekiel 48:30-35 KJV

'And these are the goings out of the city on the north side, four thousand and five hundred measures. And the gates of the city shall be after the names of the tribes of Israel: three gates northward; one gate of Reuben, one gate of Judah, one gate of Levi. And at the east side four thousand and five hundred: and three gates; and one gate of Joseph, one gate of Benjamin, one gate of Dan. And at the south side four thousand and five hundred measures: and three gates; one gate of Simeon, one gate of Issachar, one gate of Zebulun. At the west side four thousand and five hundred, with their three gates; one gate of Gad, one gate of Asher, one gate of Naphtali. It was round about eighteen thousand measures: and the name of the city from that day shall be, **The LORD is there** .' Ezekiel 48:30-35 KJV

God is omnipresent, meaning he is all present. When Jesus died on the Cross, scripture says he tore the veil in the temple (Matthew 27:50-51, Mark 15:38, Luke 23:45). This is very significant because on one side of the veil, a person would come into the temple to talk to God through a high priest who sat on the other side. Jesus became our High Priest and tore the veil, so no matter where we are, we can call on the name of the LORD, Jesus Christ.

I will leave you with this: some people will claim that Jesus is not God, but He is separate, that He is only the Son. Countless scriptures speak of the Oneness of God and counter this claim. Jesus is not only the Son, but He is also the Father, and He is also the Holy Spirit. So when Jesus says we must be baptized in the name of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit (Matthew 28:19), the name we must be baptized in is Jesus Christ, just as Peter instructs in Acts 2:38.

I know there was a lot here to digest —much of the Hebrew language and scripture —but my hope is that we better understand who God is through this discussion and are encouraged to learn more through self-study. Take the time to pray and ask God to reveal more of Himself to you through devotion and prayer.

## The LORD Gives and the LORD Takes Away
**Published:** 2025-11-06 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/10/the-lord-gives-lord-takes-away.html)**

---

“He said, “Naked I came from my mother’s womb, and naked will I return there. The LORD gave, and the LORD has taken away; blessed be the name of the LORD.”” Job 1:21 MEV

Job notes that the LORD gives and the LORD takes away.

So often, we highlight that God is a provider; He is Jehovah-jireh, meaning "The LORD will provide" (Genesis 22).

I do not want to discredit how God gives to His people, but I must let it be known that God also takes away.

'You have turned my mourning into dancing; You have taken off my sackcloth and girded me with gladness,' Psalms 30:11 MEV

'You have turned my mourning into dancing; You have taken off my sackcloth and girded me with gladness,' Psalms 30:11 MEV

Before we can dance, we must first have our mourning taken away; the only One who can do this is God. The Psalmist, referring to God, has said, " **You have taken off my sackcloth and girded me with gladness** ." The transaction taking place from God is the removal of sorrow and the replacement with joy. The LORD gives and the LORD takes away.

'But we all are as an unclean thing, and all our righteousness is as filthy rags; and we all fade as a leaf, and our iniquities, like the wind, have taken us away.' Isaiah 64:6 MEV

'But we all are as an unclean thing, and all our righteousness is as filthy rags; and we all fade as a leaf, and our iniquities, like the wind, have taken us away.' Isaiah 64:6 MEV

We are all unclean, stained with the sin brought on from Adam and Eve. We were born into sin, but sin is not the end; the prophet Isaiah also declares:

'I will greatly rejoice in the LORD, my soul shall be joyful in my God; for He has clothed me with the garments of salvation, He has covered me with the robe of righteousness, as a bridegroom decks himself with ornaments, and as a bride adorns herself with her jewels. ' Isaiah 61:10 MEV

'I will greatly rejoice in the LORD, my soul shall be joyful in my God; for He has clothed me with the garments of salvation, He has covered me with the robe of righteousness, as a bridegroom decks himself with ornaments, and as a bride adorns herself with her jewels. ' Isaiah 61:10 MEV

The only way we can go from filthy rags to garments of salvation and robes of righteousness is if the LORD has taken away the old things and replaced them with the new.

'Therefore, if any man is in Christ, he is a new creature. Old things have passed away. Behold, all things have become new. **All this is from God** , who has reconciled us to Himself through Jesus Christ and has given to us the ministry of reconciliation, that is, that God was in Christ reconciling the world to Himself, not counting their sins against them, and has entrusted to us the message of reconciliation.' 2 Corinthians 5:17-19 MEV

'Therefore, if any man is in Christ, he is a new creature. Old things have passed away. Behold, all things have become new. **All this is from God** , who has reconciled us to Himself through Jesus Christ and has given to us the ministry of reconciliation, that is, that God was in Christ reconciling the world to Himself, not counting their sins against them, and has entrusted to us the message of reconciliation.' 2 Corinthians 5:17-19 MEV

Notice, " **All this is from God** ," which demonstrates once more that our God gives and takes away; He makes us a new creature by taking away the old man.

In all of these cases, when the LORD gives, He is also taking away at the same time. This is the case in every exchange with God, even when we may not understand the gain in times of loss.

“Behold, I have created the smith who blows the coals in the fire and who brings forth an instrument for his work; and I have created the destroyer in order to cause ruin.” Isaiah 54:16 MEV

“Behold, I have created the smith who blows the coals in the fire and who brings forth an instrument for his work; and I have created the destroyer in order to cause ruin.” Isaiah 54:16 MEV

This verse is from the prophet Isaiah, through whom God is speaking, so God says He created the destroyer (the King James Version says the waster). So in some cases, maybe God isn't taking away directly, but instead indirectly.

This is not to say that every loss comes from God, but don't think just because something is being taken away that it is an act of the Enemy.

There will be losses in life that are meant to strengthen us. As we go through life, some doors will open — and praise God for that — but some will also close — and praise God for that. What I mean is, not every opportunity that comes our way is from God, and many of the opportunities we miss may just be God's Will. In the case of jobs, we will not get every job that we apply for, and if we don't, then we must ask ourselves, *Was that really God's Will for me?* Maybe we would've been overburdened or lost the ability to attend church with this career. In the case of friendships, when people walk out on us and leave, we have to ask: *Was that person* * a positive contribution to my spiritual life?* Whether they were someone who edified you or allowed you to edify them and strengthen their walk with God, there should be some sharpening of countenance (Proverbs 27:17).

I'll leave you with this: If I had accepted every opportunity that came my way, I would be in all six branches of the military and attending dozens of colleges across North America. Again, not every opportunity is from God; we need to pray and ask for discernment between things of God and things outside His Plan for our lives.

I want to pause here to let you know we are about to enter a heavy part of this discussion.

In December 2023, I lost my grandma to lung cancer and lung disease. I was 18 years old and had only attended one family member's funeral before this, but I did not know them very well and was very young when they passed.

In June 2025, I lost my aunt to pancreatic cancer. It felt too soon to lose another family member, especially this aunt who always took the burden of hosting all of the family events and preparing the food, even before grandma had passed away.

In October 2025, I lost four of my cousins in a helicopter accident. I had felt that losing my aunt after my grandma was too soon, but that did not compare to losing my two older cousins and their kids in a tragic accident only four months later.

I have had my fair share of losses, and I know I am not the only one. Many people have had losses even greater than mine, but at the end of the day, we must understand that God is in control.

Despite these times of loss, I have gained so much. I have a hope that one day all of us will be rejoicing in Heaven together. I have joy that, through all the individuals I lost, God used those opportunities to bring families together and to touch the lives of those in the room who had not yet surrendered themselves to God. Most notably, I have a faith that is stronger than ever, that no matter what storm comes my way, I trust in the God who controls the wind and the waves, the God who walks by my side in the fiery furnace.

___________________________________________________________________________

No matter what happens in our lives, God is omnipotent, meaning all-powerful. Verses like this one in Job teach us this:

“Then Job answered the LORD and said: “I know that You can do everything, and that no thought can be withheld from You.”” Job 42:1-2 MEV

Keep in mind that this verse comes from a man who had everything stripped away from him, yet he still chose to serve the LORD, believing in God's power despite his own storm.

These verses from the Psalms remind us that God has created this earth, and therefore is sovereign:

“The earth is the LORD’s, and the fullness thereof, the world, and those who dwell therein. For He has founded it on the seas, and established it on the rivers.” Psalms 24:1-2 MEV

“The LORD has established His throne in the heavens, and His kingdom rules over all.” Psalms 103:19 MEV

God created the entire universe, and He will forever have dominion over what happens. God is in control; He has the final say.

___________________________________________________________________________

I can't say I fully understand how God works; His thoughts and His ways far exceed mine (Isaiah 55:8-9), and we shouldn't always try to understand God's reasoning for what happens or why, as that undermines faith. We are called to walk by faith, not by sight (2 Corinthians 5:7), meaning we won't always see or understand why things happen, but we can have assurance that God is working all things for our good (Romans 8:28).

## Faith Within the Storm
**Published:** 2025-10-30 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/10/faith-within-storm.html)**

---

In this discussion, I want to study what the Bible says about faith.

“Now faith is the substance of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen.” Hebrews 11:1 MEV

Faith, the Bible tells us, is believing without seeing. The world will say things like “seeing is believing” or “I’ll believe it when I see it,” but those are contrary to the Word of God. Paul confirms this once more in his other epistle.

“while we do not look at the things which are seen, but at the things which are not seen. For the things which are seen are temporal, but the things which are not seen are eternal.” 2 Corinthians 4:18 MEV

I can see the leaves on a tree, but they have already begun to fall and wither. Those leaves that I see are temporal; they are only there for a season. When it comes to wind, I can see only the effects thereof; I can't honestly see wind itself.

“The wind blows where it wishes, and you hear its sound, but you do not know where it comes from or where it goes. So it is with everyone who is born of the Spirit.”” John 3:8 MEV

The Bible compares the Spirit of God to wind; in fact, the Greek word used here for wind, *pneuma,* is also used when referring to the Spirit of God. Both the wind and God are eternal and go unseen. Unlike the leaves, the wind isn't only for a season; it remains in every season, though it may be more prominent in some. There will be times in our lives when God feels much nearer to us, and other times when we find ourselves asking, *God, where did you go?* We see this in the story of Job.

'“Behold, I go forward, but He is not there, and backward, but I cannot perceive Him; when He works on the left hand, I cannot behold Him; He turns to the right hand, I cannot see Him.' Job 23:8-9 MEV

Job is at a loss; the One he served all his life seems beyond his reach now. However, as readers of Job, we understand that God was with Job all along, testing his faithfulness.

'Be strong and of a good courage. Fear not, nor be afraid of them, for the Lord your God, it is He who goes with you. He will not fail you, nor forsake you.”' Deuteronomy 31:6 MEV

'No man will be able to stand against you all the days of your life. As I was with Moses, I will be with you. I will not abandon you. I will not leave you. ' Joshua 1:5 MEV

God will never forsake us. If His Word declares it, then it is true. Even when we can't feel Him working, He is working all the more.

Reading the story about the three Hebrew boys in Daniel 3 (Hananiah, Mishael, and Azariah; their Babylonian names being Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego), we can draw a critical detail. When these boys went through the fiery furnace, and everyone saw the fourth man in the fire — who was a manifestation of God, meaning *He* was God — the Bible never says that the Hebrew boys saw their God with them in their circumstances.

Fast-forwarding to the New Testament, we see the disciples endure a storm.

“Then He entered the boat, and His disciples followed Him. And behold, a great storm arose on the sea, so that the boat was covered with the waves. But He was asleep. His disciples went to Him and awoke Him, saying, “Lord, save us! We are perishing!” He replied, “Why are you fearful, O you of little faith?” Then He rose and rebuked the winds and the sea. And there was a great calm. The men were amazed, saying, “What kind of Man is this that even the winds and the sea obey Him!”” Matthew 8:23-27 MEV

“Then He entered the boat, and His disciples followed Him. And behold, a great storm arose on the sea, so that the boat was covered with the waves. But He was asleep. His disciples went to Him and awoke Him, saying, “Lord, save us! We are perishing!” He replied, “Why are you fearful, O you of little faith?” Then He rose and rebuked the winds and the sea. And there was a great calm. The men were amazed, saying, “What kind of Man is this that even the winds and the sea obey Him!”” Matthew 8:23-27 MEV

We can read in the other Gospels and notice that every account describes the disciples' bewilderment at Jesus' power and authority.

“They feared greatly and said to one another, “What kind of Man is He, that even the wind and the sea obey Him?”” Mark 4:41 MEV

“They feared greatly and said to one another, “What kind of Man is He, that even the wind and the sea obey Him?”” Mark 4:41 MEV

“He said to them, “Where is your faith?” Being afraid, they marveled, saying to each other, “Who then is this Man? He commands even the winds and water, and they obey Him.”” Luke 8:25 MEV

“He said to them, “Where is your faith?” Being afraid, they marveled, saying to each other, “Who then is this Man? He commands even the winds and water, and they obey Him.”” Luke 8:25 MEV

It was at this moment that Jesus' disciples began to see Him for who He really was. People during Jesus' time believed that He was John the Baptist, Elijah, Jeremiah, or another prophet. But when Jesus asked His disciples who they believed Him to be eight chapters after the storm, Peter said with boldness that Jesus was " **the Christ, the Son of the living God** " (Matthew 16:13-16). Peter was able to declare this because he had gotten to know Jesus personally during that storm; the hardship had revealed to Him that Jesus was God, not just a prophet sent by God.

Looking at one last person in this discussion, we can read about John the Baptist and the faith that he carried. We read about John and Jesus' meeting, and it goes like this:

'The next day John saw Jesus coming toward him and said, “Behold, the Lamb of God, who takes away the sin of the world! This is He of whom I said, ‘After me comes a Man who is preferred before me, for He was before me.’ I did not know Him, but for this reason I came baptizing with water: so that He might be revealed to Israel.” ' John 1:29-31 MEV

'The next day John saw Jesus coming toward him and said, “Behold, the Lamb of God, who takes away the sin of the world! This is He of whom I said, ‘After me comes a Man who is preferred before me, for He was before me.’ I did not know Him, but for this reason I came baptizing with water: so that He might be revealed to Israel.” ' John 1:29-31 MEV

Before Peter had declared Jesus to be who He was, John the Baptist did it first. It reads as if the two had not even spoken a word, and just by seeing Jesus, John knew Him for who He was. John was a prophet, paving the way for Jesus to show, but unlike the previous prophets, he was able to see God with whom he communicated with, *in the flesh* .

I want to look at another piece of scripture about John the Baptist. In Luke 7, John hears from his disciples about the works of Jesus, the miracles he had been performing throughout the land. So John sends his disciples to Jesus to ask, " **Are You the One who is coming, or shall we look for another?** "

Okay, something doesn't add up here. The same John the Baptist who shouted, " **Behold, the Lamb of God** ," now wants to know if Jesus is the real deal. So what happened? When did John's faith suddenly leave him? To give some context, John was in a storm. King Herod of Judea, who ruled Jerusalem and Bethlehem, had imprisoned John, leaving him with uncertainity and doubt.

It is in the storm that our faith is either broken or refined, and in the midst of the storm, we have to decide which it will be. When we find ourselves struggling in our faith because we don't see a way out, hold onto the fact that the God we serve is a waymaker. If you need healing, physically or mentallly, talk to the God who gives the blind their sight, who delivers men and women from addiction and mental illness, and the God who is none other than the great I AM. Let us take the time to pray before the storm arises.

___________________________________________________________________________

'When Jesus came into the region of Caesarea Philippi, He asked His disciples, “Who do men say that I, the Son of Man, am?” They said, “Some say that You are John the Baptist, others say Elijah, and others Jeremiah or one of the prophets.” He said to them, “But who do you say that I am?” Simon Peter replied, “You are the Christ, the Son of the living God.” ' Matthew 16:13-16 MEV

'When Jesus came into the region of Caesarea Philippi, He asked His disciples, “Who do men say that I, the Son of Man, am?” They said, “Some say that You are John the Baptist, others say Elijah, and others Jeremiah or one of the prophets.” He said to them, “But who do you say that I am?” Simon Peter replied, “You are the Christ, the Son of the living God.” ' Matthew 16:13-16 MEV

'The disciples of John told him of all these things. John, calling for two of his disciples, sent them to Jesus, saying, “Are You the One who is coming, or shall we look for another?” When the men had come to Him, they said, “John the Baptist has sent us to You, saying, ‘Are You the One who is coming, or shall we look for another?’ ”' Luke 7:18-20 MEV

'The disciples of John told him of all these things. John, calling for two of his disciples, sent them to Jesus, saying, “Are You the One who is coming, or shall we look for another?” When the men had come to Him, they said, “John the Baptist has sent us to You, saying, ‘Are You the One who is coming, or shall we look for another?’ ”' Luke 7:18-20 MEV

## The Sower
**Published:** 2025-10-16 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/09/the-good-sower.html)**

---

I wanted to take the time in this discussion to study the Parable of the Sower told by Jesus in Luke chapter 8.

'A sower went out to sow his seed: and as he sowed, some fell by the way side ; and it was trodden down, and the fowls of the air devoured it. And some fell upon a rock ; and as soon as it was sprung up, it withered away, because it lacked moisture. And some fell among thorns ; and the thorns sprang up with it, and choked it. And other fell on good ground , and sprang up, and bare fruit an hundredfold. And when he had said these things, he cried, He that hath ears to hear, let him hear.' Luke 8:5-8 KJV

Christ often spoke in parables like this to captivate both non-believers and believers alike and help them carry His teachings with them. It's much easier to remember a story than a lecture; this is a method that some of my college professors excel at, which makes learning all the more attainable. Jesus wanted everyone to understand Truth, so He brought it to a level that all could comprehend, instead of only the scribes and Pharisees.

___________________________________________________________________________

Side note:

In 1526, William Tyndale translated the New Testament from Latin into English for the first time. This man took a significant risk in making the Bible so accessible because, at the time, the Catholic Church held that Scripture was to be written only in Latin. Tyndale would be burned at the stake for his actions in 1536.

The Bible was never meant to be exclusive; it was intended to be available for everyone so they could better understand the God who created them and saved them from sin. Jesus' method for speaking in parables confirms this idea.

Through reading and studying the Bible, we can feel more connected to our Savior. Not only is Scripture the inspired Word of God (2 Timothy 3:16; 2 Peter 1:21), but it is God Himself (John 1:1).

___________________________________________________________________________

In the case of the Luke chapter 8 parable, we don't have to dig too far to find its meaning. Jesus' disciples asked Him for an explanation, and to that He said:

'Now the parable is this: The seed is the word of God. Those by the way side are they that hear; then cometh the devil, and taketh away the word out of their hearts, lest they should believe and be saved. They on the rock are they, which, when they hear, receive the word with joy; and these have no root, which for a while believe, and in time of temptation fall away. And that which fell among thorns are they, which, when they have heard, go forth, and are choked with cares and riches and pleasures of this life, and bring no fruit to perfection. But that on the good ground are they, which in an honest and good heart, having heard the word, keep it, and bring forth fruit with patience.' Luke 8:11-15

There are four cases for where the seed lands:

By the Way Side

Upon a Rock

Among Thorns

On Good Ground

Interestingly enough, in the first three cases, we see at work the three enemies of the soul: the devil, the flesh, and the world.

Christ explains that the seed in the parable is the Word of God, and in the first case, the seeds *fell by the way side* : people hear the Word, and the devil takes it away from their hearts. This reminds me of one of the psalms, which says, ' **Your word I have hidden in my heart, That I might not sin against You** ' (Psalm 119:11 NKJV). I didn't understand the significance of hiding God's Word in our hearts, but as a result of this study, I have learned that the Enemy seeks to take it away from us. We have said already that the LORD is His Word. When the Enemy comes in, he is trying to take God away from us. Blasphemy is considered the unforgivable sin (Matthew 12:22-25, 31-32), not because God won't forgive us, but because we have displaced ourselves so far from God that we no longer even acknowledge that He is real, that He has all power and authority; we have lost all conviction and sensitivity. Be assured, if you fear that you have ever blasphemed God, you haven't because you still hold that fear for the LORD and keep His statutes. However, those who have blasphemed God (who have denied that God is real, that He still comforts, and that He still heals) got to that point because they allowed Satan to steal the Word out of their hearts. They grew bitter in their life and denied God's existence. We can never let bitterness or jealousy take place in our hearts. No matter what happens in our lives compared to the lives of our brothers and sisters, we must always be unified, celebrating their circumstances despite our own.

In the second case, the seeds *fell upon a rock* : the people hear the Word, experience the joy, celebrate it, but they don't allow a root to form; they don't root themselves in Christ and desire the feeling over their Savior and their Creator. This is why it is so important to fall in love with the things of God. We need to be in love with going to the House of the LORD. Some Sundays, I have to be honest, I am tired and want to stay home, but we all need to love God and things of God so much that, despite our flesh, we obey the instruction God has given us. What would happen if we became so rooted that no amount of our fleshly desires could convince us to go in the opposite direction of God? To be rooted, we need nutrients; we need to feed ourselves nourishing foods. Someone who eats McDonald's every day shouldn't expect themselves to be a successful athlete. We as Christians, if we are not reading our Bibles every day and praying to God, how are we to be successful in reaching the lost and being a light? There is the saying, "You reap what you sow." When a farmer plants corn and it's time to harvest, he expects corn. It doesn't make any sense to plant something and expect something else. In other words, you get what you give. For instance, if I have an exam to study for, I cannot spend all day watching television and expect to get a good grade. Spiritually speaking, we cannot spend our time giving in to our fleshly desires while ignoring prayer and devotion, and still expect God to communicate with us.

For the third case, the seeds *fell among thorns* : the people have the Word of God, but have become so in love with the things of this world that they have begun to serve the idols of this world, so that there is no more room for God. ' **No servant can serve two masters; for either he will hate the one and love the other, or else he will be loyal to the one and despise the other. You cannot serve God and mammon** [or money]' (Luke 16:13 KJV). The world has very desirable things, all of which, to some extent, contradict the things of God. The Bible teaches that we should decrease so that the LORD can increase (John 3:30), that we should humble ourselves before God (James 4:10, 1 Peter 5:6). The world tells us that we should exalt ourselves, don't let anyone get in our way, because they won't return any favors, so why should we give them any? Money is nice, titles and positions, even fame —they're all nice —but none of them are everlasting. The world will give you all your desires, but when this life ends, or when that trumpet sounds, none of it is going to matter. We are called to be in the world, but not of it (John 15:19). We must ensure that we are standing on the firm foundation of Christ, that we don't become too prideful and think *I would * never * fall for the world's tricks. I could * never * go back there* . ' **So, if you think you are standing firm, be careful that you don't fall!** ' (1 Corinthians 10:12 NIV).

In closing, we must not discredit the fourth case, the seeds that *fell on good ground* : the people who have heard the Word of God and have not succumbed to any of the temptations given to them by the Enemy, their flesh, or the world. Instead, these people carry God's Word within their hearts; they have rooted themselves in the things of God, and they have remained so close to God through reading His Word to know Him and through prayer, that they can focus more on eternal things and the life after this one.

I encourage each and every person to study this parable further. God's Word can never be exhausted; it will speak to you in a different way than it spoke to me, and I am so thankful for that. Let's pray in the messages to come, the studying of scriptures, and through every encounter with the Word of God, that that Word will fall on good ground.

___________________________________________________________________________

## Time is Money
**Published:** 2025-10-09 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/10/time-is-money.html)**

---

Time is limited. I won't beat around the bush; I must be blunt in stating that time is a nonrenewable resource. Once used, it cannot be returned. For this reason, we must be so cautious in how we manage our time.

There's the saying that 'time is money.' This is not only true for the purposes of this discussion, but also in society and even economics. At the beginning of this semester in school, I started taking a microeconomics class where one of the first things we discussed was *opportunity costs* . Every action we take comes at a price; even when we think something is 'free,' we will still have to spend our time fulfilling it. My professor is continuously reminding us, "There is no such thing as a free lunch."

Now that we have said time is money, we must also understand that just like our finances, time is not ours but rather we are under the stewardship of God, of His gift of time. Recall the servants in Matthew 25, the ones with their lord's money who were instructed to invest it and gain an increase. One servant went from five talents to ten, another from two to four, and the last servant started with one talent and ended with the same. The first two servants were told, **"Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord."** However, unto the third servant his lord said, **"Thou wicked and slothful servant..."** I don't know about you, but I want to hear my LORD say, **"Well done, thou good and faithful servant."**

The wicked servant sat on his master's money so as to not lose it. One can argue that there was a depreciation of said money because of a lack of investment; the money was idle and was not working for anybody. When it comes to our time, we cannot just stand by in fear of misusing it because that, in and of itself, is an improper use. Doing nothing is still doing something; it's the wrong kind of something, a slothful (or lazy) type of something. If we sit still out of fear of spending our limited time, the reality is that time still passes and slips away from our grasp. So, in my eyes, the best course of action to get the most out of our time is to spend it on the things of God, the things that are going to return the greatest investments.

In 1 Corinthians 3, it explains how one day our works will be tried by fire.

'every man's work shall be made manifest: for the day shall declare it, because it shall be revealed by fire; and the fire shall try every man's work of what sort it is. If any man's work abide which he hath built thereupon, he shall receive a reward. If any man's work shall be burned, he shall suffer loss: but he himself shall be saved; yet so as by fire.' 1 Corinthians 3:13-15 KJV

All of those things we have invested our time in will do one of two things: either they will burn up as if they never happened, or they will become refined, shining bright in the heat. The things that will burn away are those of wood, hay, and stubble, works that are not necessarily sinful but have no substantial effect in eternity.

When it comes to me, I have a variety of hobbies that I enjoy to pass the time. I like to run, read books of fiction, and learn new languages; all of these things are great and all, but at the end of the day, they'll be burned up when tried by fire. This isn't to say that these hobbies of mine are sinful or a waste of time, but they don't have any everlasting effect when this life of mine ends. Now, don't think that we as people shouldn't have hobbies, because we all should, and God may even use them to advance His Kingdom. However, do understand that we need to have works in our lives that won't be burned up by a trial of fire.

When it comes to works that won't be burned up, works that are gold, silver, and precious stones, the fire will not take away these works. These are the kinds of works that don't glorify ourselves, but please God more than they please ourselves. When we take time to pray and fast, study Scripture, make disciples, or even one day work a job as a way to provide for our family, we are building up our life with works that have an everlasting effect, works that will mean something in eternity.

Another reason that it matters how we spend our time is because of a teaching in 1 Corinthians 3, which tells us that we are to be temples of God's Spirit.

'Know ye not that ye are the temple of God, and that the Spirit of God dwelleth in you? If any man defile the temple of God, him shall God destroy; for the temple of God is holy, which temple ye are.' 1 Corinthians 3:16-17 KJV

If we don't feel any weight in that statement, then we have missed the importance of it. Our God, our Creator, and our Savior from sin, wants to dwell inside us, making our bodies His holy temple. Once we accept that fact and the fact that He wants to do so by filling us with His Spirit, then we must take the next approach by surrendering to God, allowing Him to fill us with His Spirit. We wouldn't want to go somewhere or live somewhere if we weren't welcomed, so we have to welcome God into our lives. We can welcome God in by acting in repentance, apologizing for all the wrongs we have committed against Him, and making the promise to turn things around from here on out. After we have done that, God will fill us with His Spirit with the initial sign of us speaking a language unknown to us, and then by the fruit we bear thereafter (love, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, faithfulness, gentleness, and self-control).

So why is time so important? Well, that's because the time we have is not our own, but rather it belongs to God. Not only that, but God wants us to be something greater than what we can be on our own; He wants us to be new creatures, new men, so that the old man, the way we used to be and the way we used to act, will pass away. I encourage us all to pray about how we can better manage our time, making a difference that matters in eternity, and set the foundation by placing some works that cannot be burned away by fire.

___________________________________________________________________________

'For the kingdom of heaven is as a man travelling into a far country, who called his own servants, and delivered unto them his goods. And unto one he gave five talents, to another two, and to another one; to every man according to his several ability; and straightway took his journey. Then he that had received the five talents went and traded with the same, and made them other five talents. And likewise he that had received two, he also gained other two. But he that had received one went and digged in the earth, and hid his lord's money. After a long time the lord of those servants cometh, and reckoneth with them. And so he that had received five talents came and brought other five talents, saying, Lord, thou deliveredst unto me five talents: behold, I have gained beside them five talents more. His lord said unto him, Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord. He also that had received two talents came and said, Lord, thou deliveredst unto me two talents: behold, I have gained two other talents beside them. His lord said unto him, Well done, good and faithful servant; thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord. Then he which had received the one talent came and said, Lord, I knew thee that thou art an hard man, reaping where thou hast not sown, and gathering where thou hast not strawed: and I was afraid, and went and hid thy talent in the earth: lo, there thou hast that is thine. His lord answered and said unto him, Thou wicked and slothful servant, thou knewest that I reap where I sowed not, and gather where I have not strawed: thou oughtest therefore to have put my money to the exchangers, and then at my coming I should have received mine own with usury. Take therefore the talent from him, and give it unto him which hath ten talents. For unto every one that hath shall be given, and he shall have abundance: but from him that hath not shall be taken away even that which he hath. And cast ye the unprofitable servant into outer darkness: there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth.' Matthew 25:14-30 KJV

## An Undivided God
**Published:** 2025-10-02 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/10/an-undivided-god.html)**

---

Within this discussion, I would like to explore the concept of God's oneness. I know I often emphasize the oneness of God in these discussions, but I do this because that's the example that Jesus set for us.

I would like to share a story from the Gospel of Luke, specifically chapter 2, verses 41-49. Joseph and Mary, Jesus' parents, are traveling to and from Jerusalem, making the annual trip for the Feast of Passover. The Bible says that after they leave, they make a day's journey before realizing that their son, Jesus, was not traveling with their group. So they return to Jerusalem to find their son teaching in the synagogue. Mary begins to scold her son, as any mother would, and says, "Where were you? Your father and I have been worried sick. We have been searching for you." And Jesus says, " **Why did you seek Me? Did you not know that I must be about My Father's business?** " (Luke 2:49 NKJV).

Jesus was instructing us, even at such a young age, that we are to be about our Father's business, meaning we should be concerned with the things of God. One of the things our Savior was and is focused on is oneness.

When Jesus was asked what the greatest commandment was, He had this to say,

'And Jesus answered him, The first of all the commandments is, Hear, O Israel; The Lord our God is one Lord: and thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind, and with all thy strength: this is the first commandment.' Mark 12:29-30 KJV

Jesus quotes a verse from the Old Testament, found in Deuteronomy 6:4. This tells me that God's oneness is not something new but has always been true. God is the same yesterday, today, and forever (Hebrews 13:8). His opinions on oneness have not changed. In both the Old and New Testaments, God was telling His people that He is one.

Isaiah speaks about the issues with a divided god. In chapter 44, he discusses a carpenter who works with wood. The carpenter aligns the wood, marks it up, and creates a figure in which he can worship. That same carpenter will use wood as a material to keep himself warm and cook himself food. Isaiah criticizes this mindset that involves having something that can be divided into pieces. Part of the wood is being served, it's being worshipped, while the other parts are being used to serve them as people. We serve God; He does not serve us.

This same criticism can be made in the book of Exodus, an even earlier time period than that of Isaiah. In Chapter 32, Moses is writing down the Ten Commandments from God, the first of which is, **"You shall have no other gods before Me [before the LORD God of Israel]** " (Exodus 20:3 NKJV). While Moses was doing this, the Israelite people began to fear that he would not come back down from the mountain. So all of these people started taking their golden jewelry and melting it down to create the figure of a calf that they could worship. The Israelites went so far as to say, ' **This is your god, O Israel, that brought you out of the land of Egypt!** ' (Exodus 32:4 NKJV). Gold, a material that was initially used for these people's ornaments, as a way of serving themself, was now being served as the god that delivered them out of Egypt.

The people of Isaiah and Moses' time are so ridiculous as to worship the things of the world, things that can be formed and fashioned by the hands of man. Then again, the same thing happens still today. If we look around or even conduct a self-examination, we can see that people continue to serve the materialistic, that which can be seen — the tangible. People often fall in love with money or fame, desiring the power that comes with both. They idolize people, including pop stars, professional athletes, and even those in their vicinity, through the dangerous act of comparison. It's easy to criticize people in the Bible, such as Adam and Eve. How could they have eaten from that tree? How foolish was that? But if we're all being honest, wouldn't we have done it too?

We cannot serve two masters (Matthew 6:24); one will find themselves taking a liking to one over the other, which breaches the act of servitude. How is a person to serve both light and darkness when those things contradict one another? The same can be said about money: how can we serve ourselves and our wealth when that contradicts God's design for tithing and offering and His instruction that we are to have no other gods before him?

'for thou shalt worship no other god: for the LORD, whose name is Jealous, is a jealous God:' Exodus 34:14 KJV

I encourage all of us to pray and ask God to reveal the things in our lives that are taking priority over Him. Let's ask Him to reveal oneness to us, if He hasn't already, and help us to better serve Him and Him alone.

___________________________________________________________________________

## Bought Into Truth
**Published:** 2025-09-25 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/09/bought-out-for-truth.html)**

---

In this discussion, I want to highlight a verse found in Proverbs:

“Buy the truth, and sell it not; Also wisdom, and instruction, and understanding.” Proverbs 23:23 KJV

We are instructed to buy into Truth, never selling it, but holding it fast.

To be bought into something means to be invested in it. When we are bought into something, no one can take it away; buying in is the same as being sold out, just with a different perspective. We need to be bought in for Truth, sold out for the things of God. Whether it be our time or dedication, we must invest because if we don't, the world will take those things away from us. It is dangerous when we allow our time to sit motionless, unspent -- this act refers to being bought out. Contrary to being bought in, being bought out means that, whatever the currency, it has slipped away from us without being invested.

When someone is bought into something, they care about it; they value it with a high regard. When someone is bought out, they have lost interest and turned away from the things they once cherished. There is a story of two cross-country runners, who were brothers. The older one was fast, while the younger one was a little slower, but only for a short time. During one of the races, the younger brother passed the older and took home the last medal of the day. When their coach was discussing the situation, he said, "The younger was bought in while the older was bought out."

We can read several stories throughout Scripture and draw applications about how we should be bought in for the things of God. If we examine the story of the three servants and the 10 talents, we can gain a deeper understanding of the concept of investing in the things of God. Now, it's a lengthy portion of scripture, and I encourage you to read it in Matthew 25:14-30 if you haven't, but I will give an expository of what that passage says.

There is a man with three servants who gives each of them some talents, which were a form of currency at the time and place. To the first servant, five talents were given, to the second, two, and to the third, one talent was given. Each servant had the same amount of time to increase their lord's money through whichever means they saw fit. Both the first and second servants had doubled the amount given to them, to which their lord says, "Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord" (Matthew 25:21). The third servant however, did not increase at all, instead, the Bible says he went and buried his money so that he didn't lose his lord's money. His master then scolds him and says that, at the very least, the servant could have given it to the bank to earn interest on it. The servant is called "wicked and slothful" for his actions and lack of investment in the kingdom (Matthew 25:26).

While we were just discussing currency, what would happen if we took that passage more literally and viewed talents as the abilities gifted to us from God? In both cases, the LORD called His people to be stewards and invest in His Kingdom. We should not only be bought in with our time and dedication, but also with the abilities that God has gifted us with. I heard my Pastor preach on this very thing, and his call to action was to dig up whatever talent we have buried and give it back to the Kingdom of God.

This next story I want to reference comes from Mark 12, but it can also be found in Luke 21, verses 1-4. It reads:

'And Jesus sat over against the treasury, and beheld how the people cast money into the treasury: and many that were rich cast in much. And there came a certain poor widow, and she threw in two mites, which make a farthing. And he called unto him his disciples, and saith unto them, Verily I say unto you, That this poor widow hath cast more in, than all they which have cast into the treasury: for all they did cast in of their abundance; but she of her want did cast in all that she had, even all her living.' Mark 12:41-44 KJV

So here we have a widow, a woman in a time when women depended greatly on their family, and eventually their own husbands for their living. This woman had lost her husband, and scripture doesn't say how he died, but it says that despite her circumstances, she gave everything she had to God. Without a doubt in my mind, I can tell she was *bought into* the Kingdom of God. It said in verse 44 that she "cast in all that she had, even all her living." How biblical is it that she gave her life to the Kingdom of God? Jesus gave His life, quite literally, to the things of God. Even at the end of His life, He remained loyal to the Creator. Just as Jesus would bear the Cross, He tells us this in Matthew 16:

'Then said Jesus unto his disciples, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will save his life shall lose it: and whosoever will lose his life for my sake shall find it. ' Matthew 16:24-25 KJV

We need to be so deeply bought in, so thoroughly invested in the Heavenly Kingdom, that we cannot go back on our investment. I pray that you all would put in so much time and dedication to the study of Scripture, disciple-making, and the edification of others that even if you one day find the enemy, the world, or even your flesh telling you that you should stop devoting so much to God, that you realize you have done too much to stop now. Like Nehemiah in chapter 6 and verse 3 of the same, recognize that the work you have committed yourself to is far greater than anything the world could offer you.

During the transition from youth (12-18 years) to young adulthood, studies have shown that many young people leave the church. This is why discussions such as these are so important. I don't want any of you to fall away from church. I love each and every person, whether they are under the sound of my voice or reading the words that I write. Whether I know you or not, I love you, and God tells us that's how we should live, loving others as ourselves (Mark 12:30). Suppose we, as Christians, do not anchor ourselves sooner rather than later. In that case, we will find ourselves swept away by the false teachings of this world (Ephesians 4:14). To prevent ourselves from being swept away, we must root ourselves in Christ.

'As ye have therefore received Christ Jesus the Lord, so walk ye in him: rooted and built up in him, and stablished in the faith, as ye have been taught, abounding therein with thanksgiving.' Colossians 2:6-7 KJV

'that Christ may dwell in your hearts by faith; that ye, being rooted and grounded in love,' Ephesians 3:17 KJV

The question you might be asking yourself is, *'How can I become rooted in Christ?'* There are two main ways to connect yourself with God and the things of His Kingdom, through justification: repenting of your sins, baptism in Jesus' name, and asking God to fill you with His Spirit, as well as sanctification: everything that comes after that initial step of justification, everything that helps you to make yourself holy and pleasing unto God (Romans 12:1).

One of the most significant steps a believer can take is to turn biblical principles into disciplines, applying biblical teachings in their everyday life.

Principles vs. Disciplines

Bible Reading

The Bible is the inspired Word of God; it is His very breath. Jesus knew Scripture and was able to quote it, as was Satan. The difference is that Jesus was knowledgeable about God's Word as well as intellectual, as He taught in the synagogues and in more intimate settings with His disciples.

Prayer

Jesus, the Son, prayed to His divine counterpart, the Father. Prayer is our direct line to our Savior; if we aren't praying, we aren't functioning as people of God.

Fasting

Jesus spent time *alone* from others. He took time apart from His regular routine to demonstrate how we are to spend time with God and grow closer to Him.

Tithing

In Matthew 23:23 and Luke 11:42, Jesus credits the Pharisees for tithing on even the smallest of their increases, such as the herbs they grow. Once we have an increase, we should be giving a portion of that increase back to God, after all, like the three servants, we are only called to be stewards over what we have been blessed with. Verses such as Leviticus 27:30 instruct that we give back 10% of our increase to the Kingdom of God. Malachi 3:10-12 teaches that when we tithe, the LORD will honor our actions and bless us. However, I don't want to endorse a "bless me attitude; instead, we should tithe because the Scripture teaches us to.

Offering

Earlier, we read about the widow who gave all that she had to the Kingdom of God. Jesus praised her and exalted her for doing so. It is essential to note that we should be in prayer and seek a sense of peace before making such decisions.

## Wisdom Hurts
**Published:** 2025-09-18 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/09/wisdom-hurts.html)**

---

Wisdom teeth get their name because they occur around the time an individual is in their late teenage years to early adulthood, a time when they are likely gaining wisdom. It's the beginning of a whole new chapter, either these people are about to graduate from high school, are in the middle of college or trade school, or they have landed themselves a job within the workforce. Anyone who has found their wisdom teeth coming in will tell you it is not a comfortable experience. However, the same could be said about wisdom in all regards, specifically biblical wisdom.

Before discussing biblical wisdom, allow me to help distinguish between wisdom and knowledge. Someone once explained it this way, "Knowledge is knowing that a tomato is a fruit; wisdom is not putting it in a fruit salad." I have had a previous discussion about the difference between knowledge and intelligence, in which I discussed how knowledge is the understanding, and intelligence is the application. In that analogy, wisdom is the middleman, the one who helps you transition from knowing something to applying it.

Now, what is biblical wisdom?

'The fear of the LORD is the beginning of wisdom: And the knowledge of the holy is understanding.' Proverbs 9:10 KJV

The Bible tells us that the beginning of wisdom is to fear -- or better phrased -- respect and honor God. It will not be until we start to obey God's commandments that we find ourselves acting with biblical wisdom.

'For the LORD giveth wisdom: Out of his mouth cometh knowledge and understanding.' Proverbs 2:6 KJV

'If any of you lack wisdom, let him ask of God, that giveth to all men liberally, and upbraideth not; and it shall be given him.' James 1:5 KJV

Scripture tells us that wisdom comes from God.

'How much better is it to get wisdom than gold! And to get understanding rather to be chosen than silver!' Proverbs 16:16 KJV

The Word of God says that wisdom is better than silver and gold. These metals that were so precious then, and still remain precious now, are not as prized as wisdom. No matter how high the stock of these metals gets, wisdom still remains more valuable.

'But the wisdom that is from above is first pure, then peaceable, gentle, and easy to be intreated, full of mercy and good fruits, without partiality, and without hypocrisy. And the fruit of righteousness is sown in peace of them that make peace.' James 3:17-18 KJV

James teaches that heavenly wisdom is peaceful, and I would venture to say it is ethical because it considers the well-being of everyone. In one of my college classes, we discussed ethics and how, regardless of the law, companies can be unethical if they choose profits over concern for the environment and people in society. Biblical wisdom teaches that it is better to strive for peace than profits, for relationship rather than fame or fortune.

A wise person knows what is right and then has to make the choice of whether or not to follow through. Allow me to discuss a couple of characters from the Bible and their approach to wisdom.

Jonah was someone who knew what he was supposed to do and where he was supposed to go; because of this, one may think that he was wise, but they'd be wrong. Jonah *knew* what he was supposed to do, but that middleman of wisdom escaped from him when he did the opposite of what he should have. Jonah was a prophet called by God to the city of Nineveh. Instead of leaving for Nineveh, roughly 600 miles from his current whereabouts, in the town of Joppa, Jonah gets on a ship heading 2,500 miles in the opposite direction (Jonah 1:1-3). Jonah did not want to go to Ninevah, likely because he was scared of losing his life after he would have to tell the people thereof that they were living wickedly.

Acting in wisdom is not going to be the easiest choice. On several occasions, I have considered buying a gaming computer. The easiest thing to do would be to buy the computer without thinking too much about it. However, the wisest thing to do is weigh the pros and cons (asking myself, *Will I actually use this? Can I afford to buy this right now?* ). I have also heard the advice that, as people, we should wait for a certain amount of time and see if we still want to buy said thing. These methods of wisdom aren't convenient; they're not comfortable, but neither are the things of God because they oppose our flesh.

Another wise person, the wisest of all, even, was Solomon. He was famous for his wisdom, so much so that the Queen of Sheba herself came to try him, and yet he was not someone who always did what was right before God. The Bible says that Solomon, with all his wives, allowed the multitude of them to turn his heart away from God to other gods. Scripture states that Solomon associated himself with women of diverse backgrounds, many of whom the LORD had instructed him to avoid, to ensure the hearts of His people did not stray from Him (1 Kings 11:1-4).

Admittedly, it would not have been easy for Solomon to tell any of his wives that they were in the wrong for following after their gods, which goes against the saying, "Happy wife, happy life." Additionally, it can be challenging when we find ourselves attracted to someone outside of the Faith who is not equally yoked with us spiritually (2 Corinthians 6:14). However, as already stated, the wise choice will often be the least desired of those given.

'1Now concerning spiritual gifts, brethren, I would not have you ignorant. 4Now there are diversities of gifts, but the same Spirit. 8For to one is given by the Spirit the word of wisdom; to another the word of knowledge by the same Spirit; ' 1 Corinthians 12:1, 4, 8 KJV

Wisdom is a Gift of the Spirit; we have already said that wisdom comes from God, and we know that He and His Spirit are One. It is essential to know that, like any gifts, they can be taken away when misused. Just like Solomon, when he stopped pursuing God, he started making decisions that lacked wisdom. When God gives us the gift of wisdom, or any gift for that matter, we must understand that it is of God, of the Spirit, not of our flesh. It can be easy to become prideful when God uses us, just like Jonah after he had delivered God's message to the Ninevites and expected God to strike them dead for their wickedness. We cannot allow pride to steal from what God wants to do through us.

Like the verse in James 1:5 says, when we lack wisdom, we should pray for it. When something in life is weighing us down and demanding an answer, we should ask God to guide us and help us to make the decision that leads to peace. We should understand that wisdom, just like a wisdom tooth when it comes in, can hurt and will not always be what we want. Take the time to start asking God for wisdom in advance of the challenges awaiting you.

___________________________________________________________________________

'But king Solomon loved many strange women, together with the daughter of Pharaoh, women of the Moabites, Ammonites, Edomites, Zidonians, and Hittites; of the nations concerning which the LORD said unto the children of Israel, Ye shall not go in to them, neither shall they come in unto you: for surely they will turn away your heart after their gods: Solomon clave unto these in love. And he had seven hundred wives, princesses, and three hundred concubines: and his wives turned away his heart. For it came to pass, when Solomon was old, that his wives turned away his heart after other gods: and his heart was not perfect with the LORD his God, as was the heart of David his father. ## Callings
**Published:** 2025-09-11 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/09/callings.html)**

---

Allow me to start with a verse from the Gospel of Matthew:

'For many are called, but few are chosen.' Matthew 22:14 KJV

Everyone has a calling in their life. Every single person that you have ever walked by or seen from afar has been called in one way or another. Scripture teaches us that God has a specific calling for each of us. In the famous Jeremiah 29:11, it says, “For I know the thoughts that I think toward you, saith the LORD, thoughts of peace, and not of evil, to give you an expected end.” God has a plan for our lives — a desire — a calling.

An example of God’s calling I want to shed light on is that of Zacchaeus in Luke 19. The Bible tells us that Zacchaeus was “a wee little man,” a man of petite stature. Zacchaeus is what modern culture would refer to as "a short king." When he heard that Jesus was coming through town, Zacchaeus climbed up into a sycamore tree, just so that he could see Jesus — even if it was just a glimpse. After reaching the top of the tree, Jesus walked by and *called* Zacchaeus down, insisting that they were to return to the short man’s house. Zacchaeus’ life was forever changed. All it took was desiring even a small portion of Jesus, and the LORD multiplied His presence so that Zacchaeus was able to have even more of his Savior.

I'll pause here to say that if you want Jesus in your life, all it takes is faith the size of a mustard seed (Matthew 17:20) — a small amount of faith — and an act of repentance, being sorry for past mistakes and asking for forgiveness. After that, the LORD will multiply Himself in your life by filling you with His Spirit, where speaking in other tongues will be the initial sign, and then your bearing of the Fruit of the Spirit will be the sign thereafter.

Continuing in our discussion on callings, there is another instance where someone was called down. In Nehemiah 6, we can read about a calling in his life, although this one is much different than that of Zacchaeus' calling. Nehemiah was rebuilding the walls of Jerusalem; he was doing the LORD's work and following God's calling on his life. With that being said, let's take a closer look at the few men who opposed Nehemiah. Sanballat, Tobiah, and Geshem did not support the hedge of protection going up around God's chosen city; like many who opposed God, they despised His people. These evil men thought it would be clever to call Nehemiah down from his task of building the wall, with hopes of harming him. Through the power of God, Nehemiah replied with great wisdom when he said, "...I am doing a great work, so that I cannot come down: why should the work cease, whilst I leave it, and come down to you?" (Nehemiah 6:3).

Let it be known that when Adam and Eve committed the first sin, it was as a result of listening to the call of the world. The serpent convinced both of them to eat of the forbidden fruit by acting as a wolf in sheep's clothing. Satan, disguised as a snake, pretended to have Adam and Eve's best interest at heart, when in reality he had his own agenda. The world has its own reasoning for the things it does, and that is to separate us from the LORD our Creator.

The world has called out to me many times, and I am going to share two of those instances with you. When I went off to college, I applied for a couple of universities, one was twenty minutes away from home, and the other was about an hour away, but I would have had to live on campus as a freshman. I ended up getting into both schools. The problem with the second university was that I would have been completely disconnected from my home church and the church as a whole. I decided to attend the college closer to home, which I can see has been the Will of God for my life and has benefited me greatly, keeping me anchored in my faith.

On a later occasion, I thought about trying to walk on at my university for either cross-country or track. I was not an all-star athlete by any means in high school, but I knew I could put in the work to compete at the college level. Being aware that being a college athlete requires a significant amount of time, and considering my other responsibilities at the church, I made the decision to leave sports behind, despite how upsetting that made me feel.

I want to say that neither of these decisions was made by my *supreme wisdom* or *incredible level of selflessness* ; in both cases, I felt my fleshly desires going against my spiritual ones, but God reminded me that I had a calling, one that did not pertain to fame or fortune. Like Nehemiah, God gifted me with wisdom. I'm nobody special; we are *all* fearfully and wonderfully made in the likeness of God. I have been called by God, but so have you.

Maybe you have already accepted the calling that God has placed on your life. If that's the case, that's great, just make sure you're prepared for it to evolve. Initially, I started serving at church as a babysitter for toddlers. As time passed, I found myself teaching young people about their Savior, Jesus, whom I knew very little about when I first came to Truth. While I'm happy for those of you who have found the call of God, I must ask: What are you doing to answer that calling? If you feel like God is calling you to teach, what does your time spent studying Scripture look like? If you think that God is preparing you for the mission field, have you put in effort to learn cultures and languages outside your own? Whatever it is you feel called to, I want you to ask yourself, "What can I be doing right *now* ?"

The two takeaways I want everyone to have are these:

We must be aware of who is calling us: is it God, or is it the world?

How we respond to each calling matters: are we actively pursuing the call of God, or are we waiting idly, motionless, for something to happen?

___________________________________________________________________________

A note on evangelism. I heard a message one time from a speaker who was a missionary in an access-challenged country, meaning it was difficult to send people or resources there to teach the Gospel because it was illegal to do so, or to even possess a Bible in countries such as these. The speaker mentioned something during her message that really stuck out to me. It wasn’t her main point, but it shook me nonetheless. She discussed the risks of hosting a youth service, something that we can often take for granted in a country where we are free to do so. In response to her concern, her friend told her that if they didn’t try to reach these youth, then Al-Qaeda would. The same premise can be said no matter where one lives. **If we as believers do not try to reach the lost, then the world will succeed in doing so** . If we don’t tell people about the Spirit that will quench their thirst indefinitely, then the world will try to quench that thirst with alcohol or other substances. If we don’t tell people about a love that’s greater than any other, they’re going to struggle in cycles of toxic relationships trying to find that love. If we don’t tell people about this joy that we have with Jesus, they’re not going to be free from the bonds of depression that the world has placed on them. If we don’t reach the lost by spreading the Gospel, the world will fill them with its own doctrine, one that is contrary to the Word of God.

___________________________________________________________________________
