## Past, Present, and Future Living
**Published:** 2025-04-17 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/04/past-present-and-future-living.html)**

---

One of the biggest struggles that I face in life is that I am a perfectionist. This can be observed when I am doing something as simple as playing video games, constantly starting over in hopes of a better, more "perfect" outcome. In the real world, I find myself continually dwelling on the past, wishing I had said something else or done something differently. I am the type of person who lives in the past, but I can also find myself living in the future more than I should. I push myself to the limit, hoping to feel accomplished and reach goals that surpass those of my peers (this stems from my pride). The rewards I receive are never gratifying because when you live in the future, you never actually arrive at your destination. When you live in the past, a similar pattern is seen as you move further and further away from where you are trying to be.

'And another also said, Lord, I will follow thee; but let me first go bid them farewell, which are at home at my house. And Jesus said unto him, No man, having put his hand to the plough, and looking back, is fit for the kingdom of God.' Luke 9:61-62 KJV

Jesus instructs us on the danger of looking backward in life. Imagine, just for a moment, that someone is driving down the highway and has their head turned around, facing the backseat. This becomes exponentially dangerous over a long period because, without keeping their eyes on the road ahead, they have no clue what obstacles lie in their path or if the road is about to curve. On the strait and narrow, if we are not careful, we may just wander into the broader path by accident.

'And he said unto another, Follow me. But he said, Lord, suffer me first to go and bury my father. Jesus said unto him, Let the dead bury their dead: but go thou and preach the kingdom of God.' Luke 9:59-60 KJV

Jesus is quite clear that the time is now. No matter how radical His message seems, if we continue to put off God's will for later in life, we might never find ourselves in His will at all. I think that we often want to have everything in our life sorted before we endeavor to spread the Gospel to others; however, in all bluntness, we are never going to have it all figured out. If we genuinely want to focus on our future, we must focus on our present and let God prepare us for what is yet to come. Jeremiah 29:11 tells us about our "expected end" and the promises to come, while verses such as Matthew 6:11, in the LORD's Prayer, inform us that God will supply our daily bread (all that we need). So with the needs and blessings that have been promised to us, why should we worry?

In addition to worrying less about the future, we cannot dwell on the things of the past, such as the mistakes that we have made in life. The Bible says, "If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness." (1 John 1:9). When we are baptized, all of those past mistakes are washed away by the blood of Jesus Christ. Furthermore, the blood should be applied daily through the spirit of repentance in prayer. It was Paul who wrote about the importance of crucifying our flesh and dying to it, only to be born again as a new creature in Christ (2 Corinthians 5:17).

Ultimately, worrying about the past doesn't change it, solely worrying about the future doesn't prepare you for it, and worrying about either will only endanger your present. To be entirely devoted to Christ, we must let go of the past, allowing Him to erase it, and let Him direct our steps moving forward.

_____________________________________________________________________________________

“Take therefore no thought for the morrow: for the morrow shall take thought for the things of itself. Sufficient unto the day is the evil thereof.” Matthew 6:34 KJV

”In all thy ways acknowledge him, And he shall direct thy paths.” Proverbs 3:6 KJV

## Settling Too Early
**Published:** 2025-04-09 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/04/settling-too-early.html)**

---

The other day, I was doing my morning devotion, and as I neared the end of Numbers, I came across a story I had never noticed before. In chapter 32, the Israelites are wandering through the wilderness on their way to the Promised Land. It says that the tribes of Reuben and Gad had a large number of cattle and favored the lands of Jazer and Gilead, which were excellent places for such cattle. Boldly (if you want to call it that), the men of these tribes approach Moses and the other priests and leaders to ask that they might have favor in the eyes of the LORD and be granted the opportunity to settle in these lands rather than cross over the Jordan. This must have been a slap in the face to God- here were His children, asking that He give them something other than what He had already promised them. There were consequences for the corruption of these men, ones that affected the entire nation. No man twenty years and upward at the time of their departure from Egypt would be allowed to see the Promised Land. Only Caleb and Joshua, who wholly followed the LORD, would receive the promise they so dedicatedly followed. Moses and Aaron were not even allowed to bring the people in themselves after disobeying God in Numbers 20.

Another story that comes to mind, with someone having a similar attitude to the Reubenites and Gadites from Numbers 32, is the Parable of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15:11-32. The story goes that there are two sons, and the younger asks His father for his inheritance early. In this culture that the Bible takes place in, to ask for one's inheritance is almost as if to wish death upon one's own father because one would not receive their inheritance until the death of their father- needless to say, it is quite a big ask. The inheritance was a promise made unto the younger son, but he decided that he wanted the promise now. It is essential to note that the inheritance promised to the young man was not the same as what he was demanding from his father. It can be assumed that the inheritance would grow as the father's wealth increased, so if the son decided to wait, he would receive the promise that was to come —a much greater promise than it was in the present time. However, instead, he chose to compromise. I encourage you to read the full story, but the point I want to make is that a life of compromising and impatience will lead to a life full of sorrow.

It is clear that in both cases mentioned, God had made promises to these individuals: in the first case, it was the Land of Milk and Honey, and in the second case, it was wealth. No matter what the LORD has promised us, we can be certain that it will come to pass. Corinthians 1:20 tells us that God's promises are " *yes" and "amen* ." This means that whatever He has spoken into our lives will come to pass. This could be several things: a family member discovering the Truth, a loved one being healed of an illness, or even the possibility of having a family one day (no matter what the circumstances may be; e.g., Abram and Sarai). If God said it, then it is done. For the Israelites, the Promised Land was Canaan; for us, it is Heaven. If we start to compromise and become impatient with the way our lives are going, we could miss the opportunity to see the Promised Land entirely, just like many of the men who left Egypt as a consequence of sin. We do not know when Jesus will return. The Bible tells us this several times- it instructs us to "be sober, be vigilant" (1 Peter 5:8) and to be watchful because we "know neither the day nor the hour wherein the Son of man cometh" (Matthew 25:13). Maybe our inheritance is not exactly like the Prodigal Son's, but eternal life is far greater than any wealth this world could offer. We must be patient, or endure long-suffering as some Bible translations phrase it, and never compromise this uncomfortable life upon the strait and narrow for one among a broader path.

_____________________________________________________________________________________

'For all the promises of God in him are yea, and in him Amen, unto the glory of God by us.' 2 Corinthians 1:20 KJV

'For all the promises of God in him are yea, and in him Amen, unto the glory of God by us.' 2 Corinthians 1:20 KJV

'For I know the thoughts that I think toward you, saith the LORD, thoughts of peace, and not of evil, to give you an expected end.' Jeremiah 29:11 KJV

'For I know the thoughts that I think toward you, saith the LORD, thoughts of peace, and not of evil, to give you an expected end.' Jeremiah 29:11 KJV

## Blessed Are The Persecuted
**Published:** 2025-04-01 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/04/blessed-are-persecuted.html)**

---

I want to start off by sharing some lyrics from the song "love you to death" by Josiah Queen.

I've read the pages of all those who died

In the underground church, yet you'll find they're alive

And all of them say with that look in their eyes

You could take it all, take my life, give me Christ

I enjoy listening to this song because it relays such an important teaching. Jesus Christ came down to earth so that He could be killed- by humanity's hands- and pay the ultimate price for sin: Himself. Oftentimes I wish I lived a life as radical as Jesus. He quite literally gave all that He could for the Kingdom, from life to death. I want to be a living sacrifice as described in Romans 12:1. I have heard it described that, unlike the sacrifices from the Old Testament, a living sacrifice can get up from the altar whenever they desire. This is what makes the life of a faithful believer so powerful, the fact they have free choice and still use their life to glorify God.

Daniel was confronted with a choice when the decree from King Darius came about. He could either continue praising God and face the charges for his unlawful act, or he could step away and live a safe, comfortable life. Daniel did not back down however, he entered his prayer closet, as he did day after day- in a humble manner and not as the Pharisees did throughout the streets- and was caught breaking the law. For his disobedience to the world, he was thrown into a den of lions, but never did he turn his back on God.

The three Hebrew boys — Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego — refused to bow to King Nebuchadnezzar's idol of gold. Unlike King Darius, Nebuchadnezzar was happy to punish the believers and even cast them into a fiery furnace. But even then, the boys did not turn away from God, and they were rewarded for their faithfulness when He manifested Himself as the fourth man in that fire.

Even in the New Testament, we read about the persecution that the disciples faced in the book of Acts, chapter 5, for teaching about the name of Jesus Christ. They were thrown out of the temple by the officials thereof, only after being beaten. Instead of counting their losses, however, the disciples *rejoiced* that they could receive such a beating for the glory of God. I once heard it said that we should rejoice when we are tempted; in fact, James the son of Mary and brother of Jesus, writes the following:

'My brethren, count it all joy when ye fall into divers temptations; knowing this, that the trying of your faith worketh patience. But let patience have her perfect work, that ye may be perfect and entire, wanting nothing.' James 1:2-4

Note that we are not to be joyous for falling into sin, but for the temptation that comes our way. If the Enemy is trying to tempt us and force us to lose our footing, that is because he fears us. Satan's whole agenda is to try to prove that humanity is not worth God's attention, he has been jealous ever since the creation of Adam and Eve which is why he tempted them in the Garden of Eden- he is wishing for our downfall. Even in Job's life, he did everything he could to turn God's 'perfect' follower away from Him. Still, no matter what happened in the lives of Adam and Eve, or in the life of Job, they all taught their families to live for God because they held the same mentality- even though they did not know the name of their God- they continued to live a life that spoke, "Give me Christ."

So whether we find ourselves in a fiery furnace, a lion's den, or beaten by the hands of the government, we must never stop worshipping our Savior, and continue carrying out that same attitude- that no matter what, we too will say "Give me Christ."

'Blessed are they which are persecuted for righteousness' sake: for their's is the kingdom of heaven. ' Matthew 5:10

'And [the disciples] departed from the presence of the council, rejoicing that they were counted worthy to suffer shame for his name. ' Acts 5:41

## Our Image Matters
**Published:** 2025-03-24 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/03/our-image-matters.html)**

---

There is a typical mentality in our society that goes along the lines of, ' *I don't care what others think* .' To some extent, I believe this to be biblically sound. As Christians, we need to live the kind of life that is pleasing to God, despite how outdated or undesirable it may seem in the modern day; we should never try to compromise our walk with God for the sake of others, no matter how many times they may criticize our lifestyle. Additionally, we need to understand that we are "fearfully and wonderfully made" (Psalm 139:14). It is so easy, especially as a society so immersed with social media and influencers, to think that we are not attractive enough or good enough in this world when Christ gave His life for our sake. He would not have done that if He did not think we were *enough* .

What I want to focus on is the mentality that starts out as ' *I do not care what others think of me* ,' but becomes not caring about others entirely. This is devastating not just to one's own relationship with Jesus Christ, but also to another's. Living a life that says we do not care about others reflects poorly on our Creator.

'So God created man in his own image, in the image of God created he him; male and female created he them.' Genesis 1:27 KJV

'So God created man in his own image, in the image of God created he him; male and female created he them.' Genesis 1:27 KJV

God created mankind in His own image. If we ruin our image, we also damage the image of Christ to others. People will be quick to criticize our actions, especially if it means their own views of God are fulfilled when we stumble. I am not saying to do the impossible and be perfect, but we must at least strive to be more like Jesus. When Christ came to earth in the form of a baby, He showed us the kind of life we are to live. Never did He offend anyone because He did not care about them or their opinions of Him, but rather everything He did was because of His love for each and every individual. Even when Jesus was being arrested and Peter cut off the ear of Malchus, Jesus healed the man and scolded Peter for his brutality (Luke 22:50-51; John 18:10-11).

'And Jesus answered him, The first of all the commandments is, Hear, O Israel; The Lord our God is one Lord: and thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind, and with all thy strength: this is the first commandment. And the second is like, namely this, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself. There is none other commandment greater than these.' Mark 12:29-31

'And Jesus answered him, The first of all the commandments is, Hear, O Israel; The Lord our God is one Lord: and thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind, and with all thy strength: this is the first commandment. And the second is like, namely this, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself. There is none other commandment greater than these.' Mark 12:29-31

There were two commandments that Jesus held higher than any other, to firstly love, and by loving, obey the LORD, and secondly, to love our neighbors as ourselves. An attitude that lacks caution for one's own image often comes with some sort of hatred for our brothers and sisters in Christ. We think to ourselves, ' *I do not care what they think of me; I will do what I want* ,' and we do so with disregard for their feelings.

Sometimes we become so self-righteous that we develop an inflated ego, leading us to believe everything we do makes us superior to those around us, whether believers or non-believers. This act is one of pride, and to paraphrase Christian artist Josiah Queen, pride is one of those things that the moment we begin to think we are not affected by it, is the moment we have become blind to it. Falling into self-righteousness is an easy stumbling block; if you ask any honest minister, they will tell you that even they can find their carnality rising up at times before they begin to crucify it. For this reason, along with many others, we should be sensitive to the Spirit of God in our lives so as not to say or do something that would tarnish our image and turn people away from us or from God. As scary as it may be, some people do not believe in God, and we need to be the salt and light to lead them back to their Creator.


---

## The Strait And Narrow
**Published:** 2025-03-16 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/03/the-strait-and-narrow.html)**

---

'Enter ye in at the strait gate: for wide is the gate, and broad is the way, that leadeth to destruction, and many there be which go in thereat: because strait is the gate, and narrow is the way, which leadeth unto life, and few there be that find it.' Matthew 7:13-14 KJV

This piece of scripture is quoted and referenced by Christians and non-Christians alike, and yet it can be so difficult to fully grasp.

The Path Not So Straight

Strait (as an adjective) means tight, confined, or difficult--it is significant to notice that the word is not *straight* . This distinction can help us understand that this path with God is not always as simple as walking in one direction. Oftentimes, we try to coast through our walks with God, allowing ourselves to be asleep in the spirit rather than adhering to the instructions in 1 Peter 5:8, which says to "'Be sober, be vigilant; because your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour: '

The Path Full of Obstacles

Throughout the strait and narrow, one should be certain that the Enemy does indeed seek to devour travelers trying to embark on a spiritual walk with God. Scripture tells us that this path is found by few, meaning it has likely begun to become overgrown with vegetation such as vines and tree roots. We will likely feel this uncomfortable sensation walking on the strait and narrow, as it can be very claustrophobic being surrounded by leaves that encumber our every move, and stumbling on the roots beneath our feet, causing us to lose our footing. Discomfort is a recurring theme as we will soon realize during our walks with God.

The Path Less Traveled On

Many critics will argue that a walk with God is outdated and impractical in the modern era —that it does not make sense to adhere to the writings of men of old (although they are the inspired Word of God; 2 Timothy 3:16). To some extent, these individuals would be correct. The concept of walking the strait and narrow when there is the option of taking the wide and broad is not very logical, at least at first. If we solely focus on what makes sense in the natural, we will miss the mark that God has designed for us. This is why we must look toward the supernatural; Christ tells us that the broad path, while trodden by many who move comfortably through it, will lead to destruction. It is almost second nature for us as people to follow after the trends and norms of society, but doing so conflicts with the strong principle of God's Word that we are to be in the world but not of it. Walking the strait and narrow, we will undoubtedly come across people walking on the broader path — seeing them through the trees — but we must stick to the path that has been directed by God.

The Path And Its Significance

I set out on this study of the strait and narrow to answer the simple question of, *Why does life have to be difficult?* In prayer, I came across a thought that helped me understand the answer. I began to think about the Olympics and the dedication and discipline that it takes to be like one of those athletes. Why do they do what they do? What do they receive? Well, the winners of first place receive Olympic gold medals as their prize. They dedicate their lives to a piece of hardware. Comparing ourselves to others as Christians, how much time do we devote to our walks with God? We may not receive a medal or public recognition, but we will obtain a much greater gift than that. The reason that life is so challenging and exhausting, both in our secular and spiritual endeavors, is that at the end of this life, we have the opportunity to spend an eternity with our savior. The same material that those medals are made of is used in the composition of the streets that we walk upon; something so highly cherished here on earth is the lowliest of lows up in Heaven. Life is not easy because the reward is far too great for anyone to obtain without the dedication and discipline that come with forming a relationship with Jesus Christ.

## Rest
**Published:** 2025-02-27 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/02/rest.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/27/25)

For tonight’s lesson, I have to be transparent and reveal the irony regarding it. I want to talk about rest and its biblical significance. One reason this is ironic is because I was just asking for advice about rest and what it is. Another reason is that those around me advise that I slow down and they worry I will overdo it- and frankly they are right. I find myself burning out- or lacking motivation for the things I typically enjoy- on a weekly-basis. But this only proves furthermore that rest is important.

The origins of rest in the Bible can be found within the very beginning, in the book of Genesis.

'Thus the heavens and the earth were finished, and all the host of them. And on the seventh day God ended his work which he had made; and he rested on the seventh day from all his work which he had made. And God blessed the seventh day, and sanctified it: because that in it he had rested from all his work which God created and made.' Genesis 2:1-3

'Thus the heavens and the earth were finished, and all the host of them. And on the seventh day God ended his work which he had made; and he rested on the seventh day from all his work which he had made. And God blessed the seventh day, and sanctified it: because that in it he had rested from all his work which God created and made.' Genesis 2:1-3

There are a couple key points I would like to make about this verse:

God decided to rest. Now, if God- creator of the universe and the entirety of existence- decided to rest, how much more do we- His mere creations- need to rest.

God decided to rest.

Now, if God- creator of the universe and the entirety of existence- decided to rest, how much more do we- His mere creations- need to rest.

There is a sanctification for the seventh day. 'Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy. Six days shalt thou labour, and do all thy work: but the seventh day is the sabbath of the LORD thy God: in it thou shalt not do any work, thou, nor thy son, nor thy daughter, thy manservant, nor thy maidservant, nor thy cattle, nor thy stranger that is within thy gates: for in six days the LORD made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that in them is , and rested the seventh day: wherefore the LORD blessed the sabbath day, and hallowed it.' Exodus 20:8-11 This is the fourth commandment of the ten that Moses writes upon the stone tablets.

There is a sanctification for the seventh day.

'Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy. Six days shalt thou labour, and do all thy work: but the seventh day is the sabbath of the LORD thy God: in it thou shalt not do any work, thou, nor thy son, nor thy daughter, thy manservant, nor thy maidservant, nor thy cattle, nor thy stranger that is within thy gates: for in six days the LORD made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that in them is , and rested the seventh day: wherefore the LORD blessed the sabbath day, and hallowed it.' Exodus 20:8-11

'Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy. Six days shalt thou labour, and do all thy work: but the seventh day is the sabbath of the LORD thy God: in it thou shalt not do any work, thou, nor thy son, nor thy daughter, thy manservant, nor thy maidservant, nor thy cattle, nor thy stranger that is within thy gates: for in six days the LORD made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that in them is , and rested the seventh day: wherefore the LORD blessed the sabbath day, and hallowed it.' Exodus 20:8-11

This is the fourth commandment of the ten that Moses writes upon the stone tablets.

Firstly, the ten commandments are for those under the old covenant, prior to the death, burial, and resurrection of Jesus Christ. Now, I will add a disclaimer, they are still important to follow as each one instructs us to live morally upright, so do not completely disregard them.

The seventh day, or what we typically refer to as the Sabbath day, is a time of rest. The exact day for the Sabbath depends on the culture. We may consider the Sabbath to be on Sunday, the day where, here in America, church services are primarily held on. In Jewish culture however, the day of worship is held on Saturday, which is day number seven in a calendar week.

Notice, however, that from the verses we read, the Sabbath does not necessarily refer to worship but focuses on a day of rest. So the Sabbath is not intended for praise or worship, but in learning about rest we will discover those are key components within rest.

'Let no man therefore judge you in meat, or in drink, or in respect of an holyday, or of the new moon, or of the sabbath days : which are a shadow of things to come; but the body is of Christ.’ Colossians 2:16-17

'Let no man therefore judge you in meat, or in drink, or in respect of an holyday, or of the new moon, or of the sabbath days : which are a shadow of things to come; but the body is of Christ.’ Colossians 2:16-17

Many of the things in the Old Testament were shadows of the things to come, they pointed forward to Christ. We read about Adam and Eve introducing a need for animal sacrifice after sin is first entered into the world. Jesus became our spotless lamb so that animal sacrifice was no longer necessary. Jesus can also be our rest if we allow Him to be.

The famous 23rd Psalm says

“He [referring to God] maketh me to lie down in green pastures: He leadeth me beside the still waters.” Psalm 23:2 KJV

“He [referring to God] maketh me to lie down in green pastures: He leadeth me beside the still waters.” Psalm 23:2 KJV

The LORD is our rest, He is the great Shepherd who makes us lie down. The keyword being ***make*** . If it were not for God, I would rest on even fewer occasions then I do now and spiral out of control- we are not ourselves without rest.

I want to diverge into a subtopic next. We as followers of God have two main lives we live. We live a secular life by attending school, work or both, as well as spiritual lives by working in ministry, keeping up with spiritual disciplines or simply carrying the correct attitude in life. So not only do we need to reset after long weeks or times of school and work, but we may need to recharge our social batteries as well. I believe it was Bro. Shaun who said, and I quote:

“Your ministry is the overflow of your relationship with God.”

“Your ministry is the overflow of your relationship with God.”

Sometimes when we allow ourselves to go too long without rest, our relationships with others begin to falter. The same is true for our walks with God. If we do not allow the time to recharge and reassess why we do what we do, we can lose focus. Part of rest stems from having a plan.

A few weeks ago, we talked about the importance of having a plan and sticking to our passions. I enjoy mathematics and learning, as well as helping others to understand and having a positive impact on the youth. It is for those reasons that I want to study to be a teacher. However, when I allow myself to go without the rest that I need, I lose focus. I begin to think to myself, *I am not too sure how this whole career path, there is probably something else for me* . That voice I hear- the thoughts I have- is from the enemy. Maybe you have had these experiences too, and if you have not yet, you will because the enemy loves to talk in our ears and lead us away from what God wats to do. When we become tired we become vulnerable and in those moments the enemy comes softly like a lion read to pounce on his prey.

This is why rest is so important. With that being said, I have relayed all this information to say this. I hope I do not lose any of you here but quite honestly, I have no clue what rest is. I can understand the significance of rest and explain it to others, but rest is different from person to person. That advice that I mentioned I was seeking after was a simple question to Tyler asking, *What is rest?* He explained how his feeling of rest is achieved by fishing on the water, but to me, I do not feel quite renewed from said pastime. It is more likely that I would recharge from listening to a book or going on a run (which might not be considered rest either but like I said, I am still figuring it out).

Before we end this lesson, can we go into prayer and ask that God would reveal what rest is and how much time it is that we should be spend doing so.

## Spiritual Wellness
**Published:** 2025-02-20 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/02/spiritual-wellness.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/20/25)

The result of someone who has worked on their physical health is the physique they have. They may look leaner/muscular, maybe they have a new level of endurance that is visible to an outsider. They likely even feel better— less bloated, energetic, and strong. They seem to be stronger than anyone else around.

When someone betters themself mentally, they can be viewed holding more knowledge. They have plenty of degrees, certificates, licenses, etc… It seems as if there is no limit to their knowledge.

Now finally, when someone betters themself spiritually, the miraculous starts to happen. It is easy to discuss the outcome of physical and mental well-being because it is simple science. These first two forms of well-being are visible on the exterior. When you exercise, you build muscle and endurance. When you learn, you expand on what knowledge you already hold and have merits to prove it. However, in the matter of spiritual well-being, one’s interior is transformed through the power of Christ; their heart is changed and they become a new creature.

'Therefore if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature: old things are passed away; behold, all things are become new. ' 2 Corinthians 5:17

'Therefore if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature: old things are passed away; behold, all things are become new. ' 2 Corinthians 5:17

It is important to know that all three forms of well-being require constant maintenance. Without physical exertion, those muscles and stamina will dissipate as the time passes. Without a continual sharpening of the mind, past lessons and calculations that a person found themself remembering so easily will become more and more distant until they have to relearn them entirely. And in the case of a spiritual well-being, without disciplines like bible-reading, prayer, and worship, we will easily find ourselves reverting back to what we used to be- repeating the old habits and cycles.

## Prayer
**Published:** 2025-02-13 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/02/prayer.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/13/25)

Have you ever asked yourself the question- *Why do we pray?*

I know I have. I have wondered why it is we need to pray when God is omniscient (or all knowing), why is it that we need to tell Him about our struggles?

For the majority of my life, I have prayed simply because I knew it was the right thing to do. The Bible instructs us to pray so it was always easier to just go along with it. However, it is important to understand more of the meanings behind each spiritual discipline in addition to following them.

The simplest way to explain prayer is to paint the picture.

There are two key components to prayer-

The King (Jesus)

His People

It is common tradition when a person enters upon the throne room of the king (if they are allowed such an opportunity) that they do so in a humble manner.

Thanks to the power of the Blood that Jesus shed on Calvary, the veil between the God’s people and Himself has been torn, allowing us to call on Jesus’ name whenever we desire. This means that we get to enter the throne room whenever we desire- a great privilege we should never take for granted.

I have to make a confession, one thing that prompted this lesson was a conviction I felt for *barging into the throne room* .

Now, what do I mean by that. Remember how I said we should enter into the King’s throne room in a humble manner? Well, oftentimes we find ourselves rushing into prayer, immediately asking God for something. This is not the manner we are meant to pray- demanding that God bless us before we give Him the praise He deserves.

'After this manner therefore pray ye: Our Father which art in heaven, Hallowed be thy name. Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread. And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil: For thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen. ' Matthew 6:9-13 KJV

'After this manner therefore pray ye: Our Father which art in heaven, Hallowed be thy name. Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread. And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil: For thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen. ' Matthew 6:9-13 KJV

Hallowed be thy name. Firstly, it is important to notice that the LORD’s prayer does not start with, “God give me this and give me that” but rather with worship to the LORD our God.

Hallowed be thy name.

Firstly, it is important to notice that the LORD’s prayer does not start with, “God give me this and give me that” but rather with worship to the LORD our God.

Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven. Next, Jesus instructs us to ask that the will of Heaven be put over our own. Too often we try live life by our wills and our desires but the Bible instructs us that Heaven is to come first. It is scary, but also such a real thought when we began to ask ourselves, what happens if we do not put Heaven first. The alternative is not an affordable option.

Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven.

Next, Jesus instructs us to ask that the will of Heaven be put over our own. Too often we try live life by our wills and our desires but the Bible instructs us that Heaven is to come first. It is scary, but also such a real thought when we began to ask ourselves, what happens if we do not put Heaven first. The alternative is not an affordable option.

Give us this day our daily bread. Okay, so now about halfway through the prayer are we advised to ask for the needs of our lives to be fulfilled. There are two key points I would like to make. Bread is an analogy for the things that we need in life, such as food, water, and shelter. We are advised to hold necessities over the wants of our lives. Also, notice that it says *daily* bread. This signifies that we should not only pray daily but that we can trust in God to be our Provider day after day.

Give us this day our daily bread.

Okay, so now about halfway through the prayer are we advised to ask for the needs of our lives to be fulfilled. There are two key points I would like to make. Bread is an analogy for the things that we need in life, such as food, water, and shelter. We are advised to hold necessities over the wants of our lives. Also, notice that it says *daily* bread. This signifies that we should not only pray daily but that we can trust in God to be our Provider day after day.

And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. Repentance is a key part of the LORD’s prayer, and it is also a key part of salvation. The first step to becoming a new creature is repentance, which is followed by the receiving of the Holy Ghost and baptism in Jesus’ name (but not necessarily in that order, though repentance must come first).

And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors.

Repentance is a key part of the LORD’s prayer, and it is also a key part of salvation. The first step to becoming a new creature is repentance, which is followed by the receiving of the Holy Ghost and baptism in Jesus’ name (but not necessarily in that order, though repentance must come first).

And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil: We are to ask the LORD to be our Shepherd, and guide us away from evil. This is also an important ask to make daily for we are fleshly creatures.

And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil:

We are to ask the LORD to be our Shepherd, and guide us away from evil. This is also an important ask to make daily for we are fleshly creatures.

For thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen. Lastly, notice once again we are to glorify God before concluding our conversation with Him.

For thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen.

Lastly, notice once again we are to glorify God before concluding our conversation with Him.

Prayer is simply conversation with God, though more powerful than any ordinary conversation. I have said before that we must stay in conversation with our Higher Power because of the spiritual war at hand. The first tactic of any enemy is to cut the communication between a group of people and their Savior. Let us ensure that we stay in constant communication with our LORD Jesus Christ.

Let us pray…

## Having a Plan
**Published:** 2025-02-06 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/02/having-plan.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/6/25)

I was having trouble sleeping the other night so I did what any ordinary person would do… I played a few rounds of chess. At first I found myself doing really good and even thought I had beaten the computer, at least until realizing I was playing a different game mode where the objective was to actually lose all your pieces.

While I was playing, I noticed a difficulty option for the computer and that it was based on how many moves ahead it could think for each turn. Personally, I can barely think of one move ahead let alone the highest difficulty which was ten. Later, I began to ponder how often in life we as humans fail to plan ahead.

I remember being in youth class and hearing Bro. Tyler, tell us that: “Not having a plan means planning to fail.”

Now, I know all of you are young and I’m not expecting anyone to know everything they want to do in life- I know I don’t. But still, it is good to have an idea of what one is passionate about- both in the context of their career and their ministry. For example, I am passionate about learning and then proceeding to teach others- as a result- I want to become a teacher. As far as ministry goes, I have a strong passion for the youth because that was the age when I learned who Jesus truly was, so I want to help others at that same stage learn about God.

It is important to not only have a plan, but having one within a reasonable amount of time. It would not do much good for someone at a retired age to decide they want to pursue a new career. The same can be said regarding ministry- can you imagine if Pastor walked up to the pulpit and asked himself in that moment, “What should I preach on today?” That would be crazy and exhibit poor spiritual disciplines- and that is why I want to talk to you all about this because being prepared is a biblical concept.

“Be sober, be vigilant; because your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour:” 1 Peter 5:8 KJV

“Be sober, be vigilant; because your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour:” 1 Peter 5:8 KJV

Peter tells us to be ready because our adversary is ready to throw everything he can at us, so if we are not prepared for it then we are sure to stumble.

During the creation, notice how it took God six days to create Earth and all its life. That wasn’t because God couldn’t do it in an instant- He is omnipotent and if He wanted could have easily accomplished said task. But instead, God took His time, He planned out everything very meticulously to ensure that “it was good.”

This brings us to subtopic of planning, we should not only strive to have a plan with our lives, but we also need to ensure we are not over-planning. I found a parable to relate to exactly this, it is not in the Bible but I believe it still has wisdom to be learned from it. There was a gardener who was very meticulous about every aspect of their garden. They created the perfect design to ensure it looked better than the rest of the neighborhood gardens. They bought a number of differing seeds to be arranged and add to the aesthetic. They even built up a fence to keep unwanted critters out. But no matter how much time and money they put into their garden, they forgot the most important thing— the fact that seasons change. They were so focused on their present understanding of the way life was going that they did not think about the new seasons to come.

If we try to plan our lives according to our own perception, we will find ourselves short of God’s plan for all of us. The LORD has great plans for our lives and has promised great things according to Jeremiah 29:11, so we need to be sensitive to the work He wants to do in our lives. It is important that we trust God for He is omniscient, or all-knowing, so He is able to plan our lives regarding not only the present season but also the seasons to come.

Earlier, we were discussing the game of chess so I wanted to share this interesting piece of information with you. The highest level chess title, higher than a grandmaster, is a world champion. Jesus should not only be our grandmaster throughout our lives- leading us day-to-day while we submit to Him- but He is also our world champion.

Jesus explains this idea in John 16:33 by saying:

'These things I have spoken to you, that in Me you may have peace. In the world you will have tribulation; but be of good cheer, I have overcome the world.”' John 16:33

'These things I have spoken to you, that in Me you may have peace. In the world you will have tribulation; but be of good cheer, I have overcome the world.”' John 16:33

Let us pray…

## New Year's Lesson ('25)
**Published:** 2025-01-02 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2025/01/new-years-lesson-25.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (1/2/25)

As we enter into this New Year, I feel it would be best to rediscover what it is we do here at Celebrate Recovery.

The Bible tells us that we have all “sinned, and come short of the glory of God”. There is a bad stigma that CR is just a place for addicts, and while many are addicts, all of us here are sinners, both you and myself included. We are all likely here for the same reasons, two of them being: $^1$to be the best version of ourselves and $^2$become closer with our creator.

'for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God; ' Romans 3:23 KJV

'for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God; ' Romans 3:23 KJV

Now, I have been participating in CR for longer than I have been attending church here at Calvary Tabernacle. Originally, I helped babysit the little ones downstairs with a friend of mine. I never imagined myself coming to church on the regular or even calling myself an Apostolic, but here we are.

Personally, I have two rules that I try to live by; the first is to be the best version of myself I can be and the second is to not hurt others within that process. Oftentimes, people are seen hurting other people to get ahead, but God calls us to be different. We are called to glorify God with everything we do and to edify everyone around us.

**Glorify** : praise and worship (God).

So glorifying God means to demonstrate acts of praising and worshipping. In a previous lesson, we discussed being a living sacrifice to the LORD, which can be found in Romans 12:1 and says:

1Therefore, I urge you, brothers, in view of God's mercy, to offer your bodies as living sacrifices, holy and pleasing to God - this is your spiritual act of worship. Romans 12:1 NIV

1Therefore, I urge you, brothers, in view of God's mercy, to offer your bodies as living sacrifices, holy and pleasing to God - this is your spiritual act of worship. Romans 12:1 NIV

This verse tells us that worship can be more than raising our hands and leaping for joy— which I still encourage everyone to continue or embark in that type of worship, but the Bible gives insight on other ways to worship God. Following the Will of God is considered worship, and God’s desire for everybody is to love our neighbor as ourself.

'You shall not take vengeance, nor bear any grudge against the children of your people, but you shall love your neighbor as yourself: I am the Lord .' Leviticus 19:18

'You shall not take vengeance, nor bear any grudge against the children of your people, but you shall love your neighbor as yourself: I am the Lord .' Leviticus 19:18

We can show love to our neighbors in many ways but the easiest way to explain the love we should show is by saying “do to others as you would have them do unto you” — that golden rule most of us learn at young ages. Personally, I enjoy receiving uplifting messages from others, encouraging me to continue my walk with God. As a result, it is important that I do the same to others by sending them a heartfelt message or even talking with them in person. Whatever that thing is that you love when others do for you, do it for others throughout the course of this new year.

**Edify** : instruct or improve (someone) morally or intellectually.

So to edify others in this sense is to teach them about God’s Word. In the Gospel of Matthew, we are instructed to do such.

“Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age.” Amen.” Matthew 28:19-20 NKJV

“Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age.” Amen.” Matthew 28:19-20 NKJV

It is important that we follow God’s Word because our image says something about our Creator. We were made in His image so we are to honor it and allow our actions to inspire others in doing the same.

I know that a new year means many new aspirations and changes we wish for our lives. Along with what we hope to change can often be followed by disappointment— but this should not shy us away from the people we want to be. Personally, it is only the 2nd of January and I have found myself stumbling on multiple occasions, but the battle is not lost so long as we get up again.

As part of CR, there is a token/chip-system. One first receives a blue chip signifying the start of the journey— it can be accepted at in any point. Following that is the 30-day, 60-day, 3-month, 4-month, etc… I encourage all of you to find something you want to be better about this year— a habit you want to rid of— and embark along with me in this program to be better followers in Christ. We will be staying for the chip ceremony in case anyone wants to start or continue working on correcting their sinful nature and collect their chip which is a testimony in and of itself.

With that in mind, let us pray— be thinking what it is you still need to give over to God and can improve in the coming year…

## War
**Published:** 2024-12-26 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/12/war.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (12/26/24)

How many here know that we are called to be warriors?

In **Ephesians 6** , Paul writes about the armor of God. He paints a picture of the typical Roman soldier but with a spiritual twist.

Belt of Truth

Breastplate of Righteousness

Shoes of the Gospel of Peace

Shield of Faith

Helmet of Salvation

Sword of the Spirit

Prayer I want to reiterate the importance of this last one. For those who were present for the guest speaker not too long ago, he said that without prayer none of *this* can work. Without prayer, we cannot see the working of the Fruits of the Spirit or Gifts of the Spirit— we cannot even experience the gift of the Holy Ghost without prayer. It is after an act of repentance and surrendering through prayer that we both read and see people filled with God’s Spirit.

I want to reiterate the importance of this last one. For those who were present for the guest speaker not too long ago, he said that without prayer none of *this* can work. Without prayer, we cannot see the working of the Fruits of the Spirit or Gifts of the Spirit— we cannot even experience the gift of the Holy Ghost without prayer. It is after an act of repentance and surrendering through prayer that we both read and see people filled with God’s Spirit.

So, why does Paul dress us up as these soldiers? Is there a war we should be fighting? As a matter of fact, there is. In that same chapter, we read in verse 12:

'For we wrestle not against flesh and blood, but against principalities, against powers, against the rulers of the darkness of this world, against spiritual wickedness in high places.' Ephesians 6:12

'For we wrestle not against flesh and blood, but against principalities, against powers, against the rulers of the darkness of this world, against spiritual wickedness in high places.' Ephesians 6:12

There is a constant war within the lives of mankind— there are these conflicting wills: 1. the true will of one’s heart, fulfilled only by the spirit, and 2. the will of the flesh— of the world— of the Enemy. This second will convince a person to follow ungodly things or even things that may not be inherently sinful but are still dreadful to God’s purpose for their life (this is why personal convictions are so important). It is crucial to not only our present-day life but also the lives of our futures (our eternity after this life) that we choose to follow that first will we talked about, the true will— God’s Will.

Now, maybe you do not know what that will is, I know I am still trying to figure out God’s calling for my life, but that brings us back to prayer.

The greatest tactic used in many wars time and time again is simple and yet so effective. In war, the enemy wants to cut off communication between those in need and their higher powers. Without the ability to communicate, there is no way to request backup. The same is true in this spiritual war. If we do not communicate with God, we are doomed to defeat, but the God we serve has never lost a battle so if I were you, I would ensure He was on my side.

Let us pray…

While I still have your attention, I want to relay this wisdom I recently read. As I mentioned at the beginning of this lesson, we are called to be warriors. This is not the same as being a mercenary. You see, mercenaries are like assassins— they fight for their next paychecks and serve only themselves. In contrast, we as warriors serve more than our own desires, we serve someone and something greater than ourselves. We do not fight for the glory or riches but for the glorification of our King, Christ Jesus.

## Passing Time
**Published:** 2024-12-05 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/12/passing-time.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (12/5/24)

The older I get, I realize just how little I have figured out about life. I have mentioned before that I struggle with pride but in general, I think in our naive youths, we believe we have the rest of our lives mapped out. During my sophomore year in high school, in my history class, I had a final exam where I was to write my own obituary. Admittedly, it was a pretty grim project, but I was under the impression that I knew everything that would be to come in life.

I wrote about how I would move to Tennessee in the Great Smoky Mountains. I had a family vacation there around age seven and loved it.

I also wrote about my profession and that I wanted to have a graduate degree in Computer Science and work as a software engineer.

I mentioned attending a nearby church in Tennessee because I googled Apostolic churches in the area- but that wasn’t at the forefront of my “big ideas” for where my life was heading.

Presently, I do not have any intention of moving ever again; I have attended four different schools K-12 and cannot be bothered to leave this community I have found myself in.

Also, I no longer wish to be have a graduate degree in Computer Science, after taking a semester of programming at EIU, I am debating on even minoring in let alone majoring.

In addition to all that, I am a lot more serious about my walk with God and my church attendance than I was at 17 years old, and I am only 19 right now.

So, I have said all of that to introduce the idea to you all that we do not always have it figured out- neither you or I have to hold a 100% understanding about ourselves or our future.

I believe I have also mentioned my perfectionism- I want everything to be planned out and flawless, but such an objective is impossible to fulfill on my own accord.

Now, with that being said, I have rough ideas about what I want in life, and I believe we should all carry said ideas. I know I want a family one day and for that I know I need a job to provide for them so I am currently studying to be a teacher. However, the more and more detailed questions I begin asking about my future, the less I actually know.

“For my thoughts are not your thoughts, neither are your ways my ways, saith the LORD. For as the heavens are higher than the earth, so are my ways higher than your ways, and my thoughts than your thoughts.” Isaiah 55:8-9 KJV

“For my thoughts are not your thoughts, neither are your ways my ways, saith the LORD. For as the heavens are higher than the earth, so are my ways higher than your ways, and my thoughts than your thoughts.” Isaiah 55:8-9 KJV

This verse explains that God’s understanding is far beyond our own. We often hear God referred to as being omniscient, meaning “all knowing”.

Recently, we have heard sermons about callings and how the Bible teaches us that we are all called to some form of ministry: apostles, prophets, evangelists, preachers or teachers…

“And he gave some, apostles; and some, prophets; and some, evangelists; and some, pastors and teachers;” Ephesians 4:11 KJV

“And he gave some, apostles; and some, prophets; and some, evangelists; and some, pastors and teachers;” Ephesians 4:11 KJV

The list goes on- there are many vital roles that few even consider ministries despite how necessary they are. We have people that clean the church, others who cook on Fellowship Sundays, and musicians- all of these people put time and effort into their talents and practice to glorify God and edify others.

The question I carry today is simple, yet powerful: *How are you spending your time?*

Now, I do not want that to sound too judgmental, I only felt encouraged to write this lesson after having a desire to watch tv and play my video games. The LORD convicted me and made me ask myself, *Am I surrendering my entirety to Him?*

We know we are instructed to present ourselves as “a living sacrifice… unto God…”

“I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, that ye present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable unto God, which is your reasonable service.” Romans 12:1 KJV

“I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, that ye present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable unto God, which is your reasonable service.” Romans 12:1 KJV

And we are also told that our bodies are the “temple of the Holy Ghost”

“What? know ye not that your body is the temple of the Holy Ghost which is in you, which ye have of God, and ye are not your own?” 1 Corinthians 6:19 KJV

“What? know ye not that your body is the temple of the Holy Ghost which is in you, which ye have of God, and ye are not your own?” 1 Corinthians 6:19 KJV

It is interesting the Paul authors both Romans and 1 Corinthians which informs us of these two concepts in the Bible. In Biblical times, when a person had sinned, they would have to make a sacrifice in the temple to atone for said sin- the shedding of blood was required. No longer are we sacrificing animals for our atonement- Jesus has paid the greatest sacrifice of all, His own life. So no matter where we are, we can commit our sacrifice unto God because we are the temple and our body is a living sacrifice.

“We love him, because he first loved us.” 1 John 4:19 KJV

“We love him, because he first loved us.” 1 John 4:19 KJV

“Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends.” John 15:13 KJV

“Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends.” John 15:13 KJV

I encourage us all here today, myself included, just as Bro. Allen preached about, we must continue knocking- asking the LORD in prayer what we are called to do for His kingdom and presenting ourselves as a living sacrifice and temple of the Holy Ghost.

Let us pray…

## A Friend We Have in Christ
**Published:** 2024-11-14 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/11/a-friend-we-have-in-christ.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (11/14/24)

Today, I want to do things a little differently. Rather than starting off with a lesson, I wanted to play a sort-of game. I have a few descriptions that I want to share with you about my best friend. As I am talking about Him, I want you guys to be thinking to yourself who it could be.

The first clue: I am closer to Him than I am with anyone else- closer than even my own brother (Proverbs 18:24).

The next clue: this friend is always helping me to be a better person by holding me accountable and teaching me how to live a better life (Proverbs 27:17).

The last clue: He not only would but has quite literally laid down His life for me (John 15:13).

'A man that hath friends must shew himself friendly: And there is a friend that sticketh closer than a brother.' Proverbs 18:24

'A man that hath friends must shew himself friendly: And there is a friend that sticketh closer than a brother.' Proverbs 18:24

“The LORD is nigh unto all them that call upon him, To all that call upon him in truth.” Psalm 145:18 KJV

“The LORD is nigh unto all them that call upon him, To all that call upon him in truth.” Psalm 145:18 KJV

'Iron sharpeneth iron; So a man sharpeneth the $^{1}$countenance of his friend.' Proverbs 27:17 $^1$countenance: translated from the Hebrew word *paneh* , which means "face" or "the outward expression".

'Iron sharpeneth iron; So a man sharpeneth the $^{1}$countenance of his friend.' Proverbs 27:17 $^1$countenance: translated from the Hebrew word *paneh* , which means "face" or "the outward expression".

‘Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends.’ John 15:13 KJV

‘Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends.’ John 15:13 KJV

Friendships are important. I took a class last year where we discussed how much of an impact there is on who an individual surrounds themself with. If one were constantly surrounded by people who cursed a lot, it would be much easier for them to slip into the same habit; the same can be said for any habit.

One of the most famous friendships in the Bible was that of Jonathan, son of Saul, and David, son of Jesse and the King of Israel. Even during Saul’s episodes of jealously and homicidal behavior (he tried killing David a lot) Jonathan stuck by his friend. It was he who warned David about his father’s plans in order to save the ordained king-to-be.

It is important that we all find friendships that align with God’s Word. It is good to be friends with those who do not fully know God, but it is important that we stay vigilant. One of the steps we talk about in CR is to help others out of the darkness but it warns us to be careful that we do not fall in. Those friendships that are surrounded around Christ and involve each individual building one and other up, those are what we should strive for.

Let us pray…

## Wisdom
**Published:** 2024-10-24 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/10/wisdom.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (10/24/24)

Last lesson, we discussed faith, today I would like to talk about wisdom. For the last few weeks now, Pastor has been preaching on wisdom during mid-week services and personally speaking there has been a lot of *wisdom* to gain from these messages (pun intended).

When we are searching for direction, or guidance, the best place to look toward is God. Now there are different forms of seeking the advice of God, all of which I strongly encourage each of you to do. We should pray, asking God to let His Will be done in our lives. There are also godly people in our lives we can go to- Tyler, Sova, or Pastor and Sister Fayth just to name a few, are all wise counsels when we are struggling with the direction. In addition to prayer however, I want to also encourage all of us to read the Word of God.

The book of the Bible we have most discussed during mid-week service has been Proverbs but know that through the entirety of the Bible we can learn about wisdom.

In Proverbs 8:17, Solomon, son of David and King of Israel, is writing from the perspective of wisdom personified as a woman. It reads,

“I love them that love me; And those that seek me early shall find me.” Proverbs 8:17 KJV

“I love them that love me; And those that seek me early shall find me.” Proverbs 8:17 KJV

It is easy to seek wisdom in times of need, but it is important that we do not wait until the last minute. For instance, when learning most things in life, hands-on learning will typically come last. When you all learn to drive, you must first learn the basics of the road before you ever get behind the wheel. Can you imagine what it would be like if we threw everybody in a car and at the last minute tried to teach them the basics?

The same can be found true in our walks with God. It is important that we seek His direction BEFORE taking up a job opportunity or choosing which school to go to.

I also want to read from Proverbs 9:10, focusing on the first half of that verse which says,

“The fear of the LORD is the beginning of wisdom: And the knowledge of the holy is understanding.” Proverbs 9:10 KJV

“The fear of the LORD is the beginning of wisdom: And the knowledge of the holy is understanding.” Proverbs 9:10 KJV

When the Bible talks about fearing the LORD, it can sound scary (no pun intended). One may read the Bible and see all the verses instructing us to fear God and wonder what it means. When one is to fear God it means they are to respect Him and keep His commandments. This is similar to the respect we hold for our parents. I love my mom but sometimes she can be scary so I ensure that I am following her instructions. The same is seen with God; we love Him and He loves us but we need to respect Him.

By learning to fear God- to uphold His instruction, obeying His commandments, and adhering to the conviction He places on our lives- we have then begun to obtain the gift of wisdom.

It is important to note that wisdom is not a Fruit of the Spirit.

We have spent some time discussing the Fruits in previous lessons and have not came across wisdom. This is because wisdom is a Gift of the Spirit. In first Corinthians chapter 12, Paul writes about these said gifts. He starts by discussing the gift of wisdom and later discusses the gift of tongues and prophecy.

We know that the sign of being filled with God’s Spirit is speaking in other tongues. This adds to the fact that the Gifts of the Spirit come prior to, and result in, the Fruits.

Once God has taken ahold of our lives, we start to treat others better than before (love & kindness), we stop seeking destruction for our lives (peace), we find ourselves showing more faith and being patient in what God had planned (faithfulness & patience).

Wrapping up, I want to share a story I heard from David K. Bernard- the UPCI superintendent's podcast. ⇒

## Faith
**Published:** 2024-10-03 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/10/faith.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (10/3/24)

Today we will be talking about faith- can anyone describe in their own words what faith is?

Hebrews 11:1-2 describes faith by saying:

'Now faith is the substance of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen. For by it the men of old obtained a good report.' Hebrews 11:1-2

'Now faith is the substance of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen. For by it the men of old obtained a good report.' Hebrews 11:1-2

I encourage each of you to read the entirety of Hebrews chapter 11 because it lists several examples of godly individuals that exhibited faith.

One of these examples references the book of Genesis in the story of Abraham and Sarah, more specifically in Genesis 21:1-7:

'The Lord visited Sarah as He had said, and the Lord did for Sarah as He had spoken. For Sarah conceived and bore Abraham a son in his old age, at the set time that God had spoken to him. Abraham called the name of his son who was born to him, whom Sarah bore to him, Isaac. Then Abraham circumcised his son Isaac when he was eight days old, as God had commanded him. Now Abraham was a hundred years old when his son Isaac was born to him. And Sarah said, “God has made me laugh. All who hear will laugh with me.” Also she said, “Who would have said to Abraham that Sarah would nurse children? Yet I have borne him a son in his old age.”' Genesis 21:1-7

'The Lord visited Sarah as He had said, and the Lord did for Sarah as He had spoken. For Sarah conceived and bore Abraham a son in his old age, at the set time that God had spoken to him. Abraham called the name of his son who was born to him, whom Sarah bore to him, Isaac. Then Abraham circumcised his son Isaac when he was eight days old, as God had commanded him. Now Abraham was a hundred years old when his son Isaac was born to him. And Sarah said, “God has made me laugh. All who hear will laugh with me.” Also she said, “Who would have said to Abraham that Sarah would nurse children? Yet I have borne him a son in his old age.”' Genesis 21:1-7

Abraham, at the age of one-hundred, and Sarah, who was ninety years old, were blessed with a child. The world would like to tell you that occurrences like this are impossible… however, the impossible is what our God does best!

Luckily for us, God works outside the bounds of logic, science, time… so no matter what it is you may be needing tonight, the LORD will provide.

Additionally, you do not even have to be completely certain of God’s capabilities.

In Matthew 17:20 Jesus is speaking to His disciples and it reads:

'Jesus said to them, “Because of your unbelief. For truly I say to you, if you have faith as a grain of mustard seed, you will say to this mountain, ‘Move from here to there,’ and it will move. And nothing will be impossible for you.' Matthew 17:20

'Jesus said to them, “Because of your unbelief. For truly I say to you, if you have faith as a grain of mustard seed, you will say to this mountain, ‘Move from here to there,’ and it will move. And nothing will be impossible for you.' Matthew 17:20

A penny is 19.05 millimeters in diameter and a mustard seed is 1-2 millimeters in diameter; so a **larger** mustard seed would be 9.5x smaller than a penny

So Jesus is telling his disciples that even if they have an infinitely small, or unimaginable amount, of faith, still anything is possible through Him!

Now, sometimes it is difficult to trust the LORD— we discussed control in one of our prior lessons— it is not always *comfortable* to surrender our will over to Jesus Christ.

Proverbs 3:5-6 instructs us to:

'Trust in the Lord with all your heart, and lean not on your own understanding. In all your ways acknowledge Him, and He will make your paths straight.' Proverbs 3:5-6

'Trust in the Lord with all your heart, and lean not on your own understanding. In all your ways acknowledge Him, and He will make your paths straight.' Proverbs 3:5-6

When we lean on what we know— when we bound our mind to this physical world and forget the supernatural power of our LORD and Savior— we find ourselves bent out of shape.

Admittedly, and as I have mentioned before, I struggle with pride which means I try to maneuver through life my way. I often stumble because of this character defect, but thankfully because Jesus holds me in His hand I do not fall. If I would begin to put my trust in the LORD and lean onto Him, I know I would be much happier and at peace with myself.

In closing, I encourage each and every one of you to give yourself over to God. He literally gave His life for us, the least we can do is surrender our desires to Him. By trusting him and carrying even the smallest amount of faith, mountains will move— giants in your life will begin to fall.

Let us pray…

## Godly Attitudes
**Published:** 2024-09-19 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/09/godly-attitudes.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (9/19/24)

“7All kinds of beasts, and birds, and serpents, and things in the sea are tamed or have been tamed by mankind. 8But no man can tame the tongue. It is an unruly evil, full of deadly poison.” James 3:7-8 MEV

“7All kinds of beasts, and birds, and serpents, and things in the sea are tamed or have been tamed by mankind. 8But no man can tame the tongue. It is an unruly evil, full of deadly poison.” James 3:7-8 MEV

The premise of this verse is that we as humans cannot control our speech. There’s the common saying that “sticks and stone may break our bones but others’ words will never hurt us.” With that being said, raise your hand if you have been hurt from someone else’s speech… It is a very common hurt to have— people hurt people and the easiest way they do so is through their words.

“Jesus said to him, “ ‘You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your soul, and with all your mind.’ This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like it: ‘You shall love your neighbor as yourself.’” Matthew 22:37-39 MEV

“Jesus said to him, “ ‘You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your soul, and with all your mind.’ This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like it: ‘You shall love your neighbor as yourself.’” Matthew 22:37-39 MEV

The greatest commandment is to love Jesus— and immediately following that we are to love others as ourself.

There’s the golden rule which I believe is derived from this verse in the Bible— *Treat others as you want to he treated* .

Jesus did exactly that, and how many know that we are to live by Jesus’ example?

“God made Him who knew no sin to be sin for us, that we might become the righteousness of God in Him.” 2 Corinthians 5:21 MEV

“God made Him who knew no sin to be sin for us, that we might become the righteousness of God in Him.” 2 Corinthians 5:21 MEV

This verse tells us how rightly Jesus lived— how perfect He was, living a life without sin.

We are instructed to follow after His image and strive for the godliness he showed. A large portion of godliness is how we interact with others; we are called to be a light to the world and it is important that we have the right attitudes.

We have all demonstrated less than perfect attitudes, *for all have fallen short of the glory of God* , but the good news is that we are all made new by the blood of Jesus Christ! Sure, sometimes we will still stumble and being tempted but that is completely normal because even Jesus himself was tempted.

“For we do not have a High Priest who cannot sympathize with our weaknesses, but One who was in every sense tempted like we are, yet without sin.” Hebrews 4:15 MEV

“For we do not have a High Priest who cannot sympathize with our weaknesses, but One who was in every sense tempted like we are, yet without sin.” Hebrews 4:15 MEV

I do not want you guys to think that I am up here talking down to any of you, I will be the first to admit that I find myself having an ungodly attitude at times. In fact, prior to the majority of you being in the youth group, I had a very sour attitude during Bible trivia (and sometimes I still find myself being hateful out of competition). When I would get a point for us boys by quoting scripture, I would forsake that knowledge by becoming prideful towards the other team. This was not something I had even realized until an older student that I went to school with visited from his church and called me out on my behavior. I was not demonstrating a godly attitude like the one Christ examplified for us to follow.

## Self-Control
**Published:** 2024-08-29 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/08/self-control.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (8/29/24)

Today, we will be discussing a Fruit of the Spirit— you know: love, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, faithfullness, gentleness and self-control. Ironically enough, we will be discussing the latter-most of those— also known as self-control.

Before we get started, can I be honest with you all? This is The Landing afterall and we are in church so honesty only seems right. Out of all the Fruits of the Spirit, the one I struggle with the most is self-control. Some people are better at cultivating one Fruit over another, it is easier for me to be kind and good than it is for me control my flesh.

I only just finished this lesson this morning after I got up and around. I had intented on finishing it earlier in the week but I lacked the self-control to do so. I would think about how I needed to finish it but other things drew my attention more like video games and television.

It is important that we control our flesh because God’s Word says…

'For if ye live after the flesh, ye shall die: but if ye through the Spirit do mortify the deeds of the body, ye shall live.' Romans 8:13 KJV

'For if ye live after the flesh, ye shall die: but if ye through the Spirit do mortify the deeds of the body, ye shall live.' Romans 8:13 KJV

The first step to self-control is being able to give up your control… Now, I know that sounds like a little bit of like an oxymoron and seems rather counterintuitive to give something up in order to better it. However, the Bible teaches that this is how we should live our life.

Galatians 5:24 says…

‘And they that are Christ's have crucified the flesh with the affections and lusts.’ Galatians 5:24 KJV

‘And they that are Christ's have crucified the flesh with the affections and lusts.’ Galatians 5:24 KJV

To crucify the flesh means to kill it and when we say flesh we are referring to our human nature— our human desires. By denying ourselves of the things we want and instead choosing the things we need, we are then crucifying our flesh.

This corresponds to Matthew 16:24-25 which reads…

'Then said Jesus unto his disciples, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will **save his life** shall lose it: and whosoever will **lose his life** for my sake shall find it.' Matthew 16:24-25 KJV

'Then said Jesus unto his disciples, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will **save his life** shall lose it: and whosoever will **lose his life** for my sake shall find it.' Matthew 16:24-25 KJV

So once again, by giving up or losing something (in this case it is our life, or our dedication to the things we live for), then we are able to become better because of it.

How many know that the Word of God does not contradict itself? It is a constant building of Truth and expands with each verse, chapter, book, etc… this is why I love this next verse that says…

'Therefore if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature: old things are passed away; behold, all things are become new.' 2 Corinthians 5:17 KJV

'Therefore if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature: old things are passed away; behold, all things are become new.' 2 Corinthians 5:17 KJV

At the start of this lesson I mentioned that we become better when we give our control to God, but not only do we become better, we become a new creature entirely. Other verses translate and say that we are a whole new creation. No longer do we have to be men and women corrupted with sinful desires and lacking a purpose; we get to be disciples of Christ living for Him, teaching others and leading them into Truth, and with Heaven on the horizion to look forward to. It is for these reasons that I plead with you all tonight to take the initial step to give yourself entirely to God.
