## Environments
**Published:** 2024-07-11 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/07/environments.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (7/11/24)

“Then Jesus was led up into the wilderness by the Spirit to be tempted by the devil. And He had fasted for forty days and forty nights, and then He was hungry. And the tempter came to Him and said, “If You are the Son of God, command that these stones be turned into bread.” But He answered, “It is written, ‘Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceeds out of the mouth of God.’ ” Then the devil took Him up into the holy city, and set Him on the highest point of the temple, and said to Him, “If You are the Son of God, throw Yourself down. For it is written, ‘He shall give His angels charge concerning you,’ and ‘In their hands they shall lift you up, lest at any time you dash your foot against a stone.’ ” Jesus said to him, “It is also written, ‘You shall not tempt the Lord your God.’ ” Again, the devil took Him up on a very high mountain and showed Him all the kingdoms of the world and their grandeur, and said to Him, “All these things I will give You if You will fall down and worship me.” Then Jesus said to him, “Get away from here, Satan! For it is written, ‘You shall worship the Lord your God, and Him only shall you serve.’ ” Then the devil left Him, and behold, angels came and ministered to Him.” Matthew 4:1-11 MEV

“Then Jesus was led up into the wilderness by the Spirit to be tempted by the devil. And He had fasted for forty days and forty nights, and then He was hungry. And the tempter came to Him and said, “If You are the Son of God, command that these stones be turned into bread.” But He answered, “It is written, ‘Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceeds out of the mouth of God.’ ” Then the devil took Him up into the holy city, and set Him on the highest point of the temple, and said to Him, “If You are the Son of God, throw Yourself down. For it is written, ‘He shall give His angels charge concerning you,’ and ‘In their hands they shall lift you up, lest at any time you dash your foot against a stone.’ ” Jesus said to him, “It is also written, ‘You shall not tempt the Lord your God.’ ” Again, the devil took Him up on a very high mountain and showed Him all the kingdoms of the world and their grandeur, and said to Him, “All these things I will give You if You will fall down and worship me.” Then Jesus said to him, “Get away from here, Satan! For it is written, ‘You shall worship the Lord your God, and Him only shall you serve.’ ” Then the devil left Him, and behold, angels came and ministered to Him.” Matthew 4:1-11 MEV

Jesus went out to the wilderness to demonstrate how to make a change.

He had just been baptized and we know after being born again we make the promise to live different, to live holy.

He went away from all of the people and everything that was “normal” to Him to create a new normal.

There are several triggers in our everyday environments that may lead to our old habits.

Remember that habits don’t have to be substances, but they can be. We all have struggles whether we realize what they are or not. It can be something simple like poor punctuality or overspending— something a little more harmful to others like lying or being hateful.

There was a study done on these American soldiers after they came back from Vietnam (does everyone know about Vietnam?). During their time in Vietnam they turned to heroine to cope with their surrounding situation. When they came back to the states where all the triggers had left, they were able cold-turkey entirely— they stopped using because they no longer had the urge to use. Even something as addictive as heroine can be broken by changing one’s environment.

Notice how when Jesus went into the wilderness, He still faced temptation. Changing our environments will aid in changing our identities, but we will still face adversity and require the help of Jesus Christ. We cannot do this on our own and we don’t have to.

Some of you may remember in Youth class that Tyler talked about being joyful of temptation.

“My brothers, count it all joy when you fall into diverse temptations, knowing that the trying of your faith develops patience. But let patience complete its work, that you may be mature and complete, lacking nothing.” James 1:2-4 MEV

“My brothers, count it all joy when you fall into diverse temptations, knowing that the trying of your faith develops patience. But let patience complete its work, that you may be mature and complete, lacking nothing.” James 1:2-4 MEV

You can change your environments to break an old habit or to create a new one.

Realize I’m not God; I admit that I am powerless to control my tendency to do the wrong thing and that my life is unmanageable.

“3Happy are those who know that they are spiritually poor.” Matthew 5:3a TEV

What does it mean to be spiritually poor?

“18I know that nothing good lives in me, that is, in my sinful nature. For I have the desire to do what is good, but I cannot carry it out.” Romans 7:18 NIV

“18I know that nothing good lives in me, that is, in my sinful nature. For I have the desire to do what is good, but I cannot carry it out.” Romans 7:18 NIV

“23for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Romans 3:23

“23for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Romans 3:23

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/29/24)

Second Principle

Earnestly believe that God exists, that I matter to Him and that He has the power to help me recover.

Earnestly believe that God exists, that I matter to Him and that He has the power to help me recover.

“Happy are those who mourn, for they shall be comforted.” Matthew 5:4 TEV, NIV

“Happy are those who mourn, for they shall be comforted.” Matthew 5:4 TEV, NIV

When we talk about recovery, it is important to remember that we are all recovering from something. I do not know all of your situations here today, maybe some of you have struggled with addiction, but I can definitely say that we have all struggled with sin.

Remember last week we read Romans 3:23-

“23for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Romans 3:23

“23for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Romans 3:23

We all strive for perfection, or at least hopefully we do- but that can sometimes be unhealthy if we are not careful. Perfection is mythology- it does not truly exist. There is the saying that practice makes perfect- well actually practice make permanent.

The closest perfect to ever step foot in this world was Jesus Christ, which is interesting when you look at His life and see all of the things he still did despite having this characteristic. Jesus prayed, He fasted- so after realizing this, I thought, *if Jesus was praying and fasting even in perfection, than we definitely should be too* .

Now, Jesus was dual-natured, He was entirely God and entirely man, so when he prayed and fasted, that was His flesh obeying to the things of God- we too are flesh and should obey to God. I may be going on a tangent here, but one thing I love learning about is mythology- Greek, Egyptian, Norse- you name it, and I believe that I love them so much because at the end of the day our God tops all of those gods. You read about these deities that force the people to worship them, but our God does not force us to do anything, because He loves us and just like Adam and Eve He gives us the power of choice.

So, we have went over that God exists, we talked about His love for us, and we know He is powerful and can help us through any trial or tribulation, all we have to do is ask. So let us do that now, we will go into a time of prayer and ask God to help us through whatever it is that we may be struggling with.

The opposite of meekness is pridefulness.

Bear Our Crosses

”Then said Jesus unto his disciples, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will save his life shall lose it: and whosoever will lose his life for my sake shall find it.“ Matthew 16:24-25 KJV

”Then said Jesus unto his disciples, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will save his life shall lose it: and whosoever will lose his life for my sake shall find it.“ Matthew 16:24-25 KJV

@ Thursday Night The Landing (5/16/24)

Review

”23For all have sinned and come short of the glory of God,“ Romans 3:23 MEV

”23For all have sinned and come short of the glory of God,“ Romans 3:23 MEV

One of the defects of character we talked about was pride, which is the opposite of humility. We discussed how pride is not always a bad thing— we should be patriotic when it comes to our nation but that does not mean that we have to believe all other nations are less than us.

The difference between these situations are that in one case, pride can build up positive emotions like gratitude; in the other case, pride is breaking down our view on others to further our own selfish agenda.

When God created Adam, he saw that Adam was lonely, even when Adam himself had not realized it.

18Then the Lord God said, “It is not good that the man should be alone. I will make him a helper suitable for him.”

18Then the Lord God said, “It is not good that the man should be alone. I will make him a helper suitable for him.”

For the sake of understanding, it is important to note that God calls woman a helper, which can also be translated to succor.

**suc·cor** : one who gives assistance and support in times of hardship and distress.

It does not say that woman is a servant to man, or beneath man like the patriarchal society we live in— or even some denominations, try to portray.

All of humanity was created equally and it requires unity for an entity to thrive whether that be on a small scale like a relationship or a large scale like a business.

Review

Last lesson, we discussed the importance of making time for God.

It is not always easy to make time for God in our lives, especially the older that each of us get, that is why it is important to start from a young age.

This leads into our eight principle which talks about yielding to God.

Eighth Principle

Yield myself to God to be used to bring this Good News to others, both by my example and my words.

Yield myself to God to be used to bring this Good News to others, both by my example and my words.

“10Happy are those who are persecuted because they do what God requires.” Matthew 5:10 TEV

“10Happy are those who are persecuted because they do what God requires.” Matthew 5:10 TEV

In order to make time for God, we have to deny some things— ourselves included— and learn to say no.

As fun as it is to spend time with friends, or play that new Fortnite season, we should make sure God comes first.

We have discussed in the past how we are to deny ourselves— and more specifically— our flesh. We have to let God’s will be done rather than our own because He knows best.

## Healthy Habits
**Published:** 2024-02-15 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2024/02/healthy-habits.html)**

---

@ Thursday Night The Landing (2/15/24)

As some of you may know, here at Celebrate Recovery and in this classroom, called “The Landing,” our desire is to conquer our hurts, habits, and hang-ups. Now, with that being said, we cannot fulfill this task alone— this is a Christ-centered program, as Bro. Shaun often says.

“26But Jesus beheld *them* , and said unto them, With men this is impossible; but with God all things are possible.” Matthew 19:26 KJV

“26But Jesus beheld *them* , and said unto them, With men this is impossible; but with God all things are possible.” Matthew 19:26 KJV

Through Christ all things are possible, and as Bro. Tyler has mentioned, for this verse to be true (and we know it is as such because it is God’s Word), the inverse must also be true: **Without Christ, nothing is possible** .

Identifying *unhealthy* habits can be easy, but what are some *healthy* habits in your guys’ lives?

## Angels
**Published:** 2022-12-28 | **[Read Online](https://jonathan-the-psalmist.blogspot.com/2022/12/angels.html)**

---

12/28/22 @ Celebrate Recovery

Introduction

In the Bible, angels were the messengers of God. The face of the LORD could not be seen, it was too much for mankind to witness. However, there were times when God spoke through other means, such as the burning bush when he was calling Moses to the ministry.

We do not know many of the angels’ names, but there are a few that we do know.

Lucifer

Many angels had tasks- they excelled in a particular role. One angel, named Lucifer, is believed to have been in charge of the music in Heaven. Although, this was only until he fell from Heaven, when his pride overcame him and he was cast into Hell.

“How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, son of the morning! *how* art thou cut down to the ground, which didst weaken the nations!” Isaiah 14:12 KJV

“How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, son of the morning! *how* art thou cut down to the ground, which didst weaken the nations!” Isaiah 14:12 KJV

Michael

Michael is another named angel in the Bible and he is viewed as God’s warrior.

“Likewise also these filthy dreamers defile the flesh, despise dominion, and speak evil of dignities. Yet Michael the archangel, when contending with the devil he disputed about the body of Moses, durst not bring against him a railing accusation, but said, The Lord rebuke thee.” Jude 1:8-9 KJV

“Likewise also these filthy dreamers defile the flesh, despise dominion, and speak evil of dignities. Yet Michael the archangel, when contending with the devil he disputed about the body of Moses, durst not bring against him a railing accusation, but said, The Lord rebuke thee.” Jude 1:8-9 KJV

“And there was war in heaven: Michael and his angels fought against the dragon; and the dragon fought and his angels,” Revelation 12:7 KJV

“And there was war in heaven: Michael and his angels fought against the dragon; and the dragon fought and his angels,” Revelation 12:7 KJV

Gabriel

The last angel in the Bible being discussed is Gabriel, who is depicted as the messenger of God. It was him who declared the birth of John the Baptist unto his father Zacharias. He also declares that John would have the Holy Ghost before even exiting his mother’s womb- and he is the only one of which that has ever happened to.

“And the angel answering said unto him, I am Gabriel, that stand in the presence of God; and am sent to speak unto thee, and to shew thee these glad tidings.” Luke 1:19

“And the angel answering said unto him, I am Gabriel, that stand in the presence of God; and am sent to speak unto thee, and to shew thee these glad tidings.” Luke 1:19

It is Gabriel who is said to cause the trumpet to sound when the time of the LORD’s return is come.

“For the Lord himself shall descend from heaven with a shout, with the voice of the archangel, and with the trump of God: and the dead in Christ shall rise first:” 1 Thessalonians 4:16 KJV

“For the Lord himself shall descend from heaven with a shout, with the voice of the archangel, and with the trump of God: and the dead in Christ shall rise first:” 1 Thessalonians 4:16 KJV

Conclusion

There are other angels mentioned in other Jewish writings, some of which we would call the Apocrypha, and because of this we do not accept those as canon, or biblical, therefore we cannot accept them as fact.

So you may be asking yourselves, *What do angels look like?* And to be quite honest, we do not entirely know. However, the Bible says that sometimes angels disguise themselves as humans.

“Let brotherly love continue. Be not forgetful to entertain strangers: for thereby some have entertained angels unawares.” Hebrews 13:1-2 KJV

“Let brotherly love continue. Be not forgetful to entertain strangers: for thereby some have entertained angels unawares.” Hebrews 13:1-2 KJV

The appearance of angels as men has been seen by several people in the Bible (i.e., Abraham and Daniel).
